## Volume 02, Batch 0003 (Chapters 71–80) — as written, Movement 3

**Volume 02 is in progress. Chapters 71–80 are written and Movement 3 is complete. This block marks every thread the batch moved, every thread it opened, and every author-level decision it made. No thread below is closed by a chapter unless a chapter says so on the page.**

### Threads carried from Volume 01, Batch 0001 and Batch 0002 and moved here

1. **The circuit office's second request and the second seal. — PAID, answered and re-asked twice, and the volume's engine.** The yard answered on the sixth of March with a **stated ground of four paragraphs and not a refusal**, on Mara Venn's reasoning that a dated refusal is an answer and an answer lets the office choose its own next move. The ground's fourth paragraph — **the yard cannot deliver a leaf of its own book because the twenty-sixth of January is the *Sea Wren's* day and the *Sea Wren* is not a party and cannot be asked** — is the strongest thing the yard has put in an office's file. The **third seal came the same morning, the same nineteen words with the first clause changed**, and the fourteenth runs from the sixth, so the twentieth of March. **The fourth request of the sixteenth asks for the sheet, the eleven copies wherever held, and the whole book, and is answered and refused by nobody, because the counter fee for a second ground is three marks and the bag was two.** A refusal is a delay; a delay is free; **and the yard is now doing to the circuit office exactly what it accused the circuit office of doing, and says so in its own book with its own name on it.** Closes across Volumes 2–4, and the answer reaches Veyr with the writ in Movement 5.
2. **The public record as a thing other people hold. — PAID as the movement's turn, and the turn is the batch.** Eleven copies, named in public on a wall on the eighteenth of March, with the place of each. **All four outside the yard's keeping have refused in a market, and the yard has not asked any of them and will not.** The seven in the yard's keeping are not delivered, and the reason is on the wall in one sentence: *a record of which there is one is a thing that can be taken away, and a yard which cannot pay a wage in March has nothing whatever to protect itself with except a thing in two places.* **And the yard learns it cannot become private again: the *Sea Wren's* master asks for a copy of the yard's own leaf and is given it, because his own log names him and not his crew, and a boy of fifteen who cannot read is a boy who was in a market and cannot be proved to have been in a boat.** The record is now in a hut at a naval pile, in a coat, in three crews' walls and a stall. **Closes nowhere. It is the volume's argument and it runs to Volume 15.**
3. **The count of the copies, out of date in two offices. — PART, and one of the two is now corrected by a third party.** The *Vigil's* slate of the eighteenth of December says **ten** and is entered in the harbor authority's ledger on the fifteenth of March as the vessel's figure, beside the yard's two dates, with neither scratched out and the difference of one entered as a difference and not as an error. The **circuit office's type was corrected to eleven on the sixteenth, in a day, off a paper of Kest's that went up the coast on the fifteenth**, and the yard learned of it from a form. **The question the yard entered on the twenty-seventh of January — has the office been told, told and not corrected, or told and left standing — is now answered: told, by the reeve, and the reeve says it cost his office something and that he sent it anyway.** Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5 and the echo of it runs to Volume 7.
4. **The supply problem. — PAID as a batch and the pump now works.** Nineteen marks of iron on the note and five out of the bag, the housing fitted **Wednesday the eighth of March**, and the pump hired out at four marks a day on the tenth and the twenty-first, which makes it the only gear in this harbor a man can hire that is not the harbor authority's. **The batch's own irony is in the book: the only reason the four-mark line exists is that the housing came up on the Wednesday, and the only reason the yard needed it is that a rival crew's pump went into a lighter on the fourth.** The air pot is topped up twice. **The *Kittiwake* is still on the hard with no cradle and the carpenters are free and have not been to her.** Closes as an income in Volume 2 Movement 4 and the iron stays owed to the chandler.
5. **The rival crew, Noll Tarner. — PAID, and turned, and he is still not a villain.** He asked for a thing and was refused in a form he can check, and **his boat, gear, pump and papers were held on the fourth of March on the ground of *entries concerning a contested material entered in the fish market of Morrow Quay*, and he read his own logbook out in a market first and put the yard's two-column practice on the endorsement, and said where he got it from, and said it was not a naval form.** He asked for the twenty-sixth of January to be struck out, was refused by the shipwright for the rule and by the holder for the truth, asked instead for the yard's leaf, and was given it. He **will not sign a sentence of Calder Marr's out of politeness.** He and his crew are at the pile. **Closes in Volumes 2–4, and the answer is the Assembly's charter in Volume 9.**
6. **Elias Voke's statement of capacity. — CARRIED, and now under attack.** It is on its way to a coast office and **on the fifteenth of March a naval officer in the harbor authority's hearing room reads it against the yard's own record and says the office ought to grant the Registry's request, and Voke stands up in a room and defends the yard at the cost of his own document**, saying that a fisher put a yard's book and a boy's name in a statement on purpose so that a coast office would know whose figures were kept, and that if a document is not good enough because a yard's figures change then every skipper on this coast will learn that the way to be safe is to write nothing. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.
7. **The charter as a leash. — PAID, and the leash is now off.** **It expired at midnight on Friday the thirty-first of March, in writing, on a wall in the yard's shed, in the harbor authority's ledger and in a file the reeve does not control.** Kest: this office cannot renew it and holds nothing that renews it. **There has been no word from Veyr and the officer of the *Vigil* said in a room on the fifteenth that he does not know either.** **Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5, where the provisional writ with a reporting clause is taken in its place.**
8. **The hard at Ashtide. — CARRIED.** Unanswered since the ninth of November and named in a document that has now been used against the yard's own record. Closes in Volumes 2–3.
9. **The ninth seam's mooring, the north berth, the warp, the third lay. — CARRIED, all unchanged and all named again on the last day of the batch.** In service from the seventeenth of February and a mooring and not a berth; the north berth out and the one that takes a north-easter; fifty fathoms of warp where ninety were to lie and forty feet of the authority's marline in the sea; **the third lay fills and the first of April is a Saturday and the fleet will want the third lay**; three tide-gang men off again from the first of April. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.
10. **A wage at four marks a week. — PAID through the first four weeks and unpaid for the next four.** **The fifth fell due on Tuesday the seventh of March and was entered as unpaid on the day and not discussed, and the sixth, seventh and eighth fell on the fourteenth, the twenty-first and the twenty-eighth, all of them Tuesdays, and sixteen marks of wage are owed on the last day of the charter, in a column with no adjectives in it.** The boy has not asked and has not been told a date. **Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5, where a boy is paid out of somebody else's pocket in part, and the arrears are not waived.**
11. **The forty marks twice. — CARRIED, both whole, both unpaid, both named in a hearing record that has now been read against the yard.** On the twenty-second of March Mara Venn says out loud that she is not going to ask for them in a month in which a form cost this harbor a pump, and that if a mark of it is offered she will walk it down the dock and put it on the wet stones. **The yard's debt to Mara Venn is not paid and may not be paid in this volume.** Closes in Volume 4 at her break, and the debt's last payment is later.
12. **The bellwright's eight marks and the going out. — PART, still owing, and now dated forward.** The eight marks are named again on the thirty-first of March as a figure **the yard cannot find before October**, and nothing in this harbor comes to eight marks before October. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5 in a scene that pays it, and the craft closes in Volume 5.
13. **Mara Venn. — CARRIED, and her break is not near.** She refuses a figure in a hearing room and in a shed; she says the words *do not make me useful* twice in ten chapters; **she says out loud that the price of the fourth of January is that the one thing the circuit office asked for cannot be done by anybody, because the person who could have done it was working in that office on the day she signed it**; she asks to be **named on the page rather than named at it**; she discloses the market questioner and his written-down refusals; she prices herself at six marks a day. **Nothing here is a step toward the break that she has not chosen, and the break is Volume 4.** Closes in Volume 4.
14. **The concealment. — CARRIED, hidden, and its third part is the one that grew.** The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation. **The false line on the unverified slate is still false and the slate was not moved.** The boy has not asked and has not been asked, for the fourth time, and comes near and does not. **And on the twenty-second of March a page was made, in the holder's own hand and at her own asking, saying that she was never asked — which is the third part of the concealment acquiring a document of its own, and it is a document about the absence of a fact and not about the fact.** Nobody in the crew asked the boy. **Closes in Volume 9 and Volume 15.**
15. **The delayed note. — CARRIED, untouched.** **No occasion is entered in this batch. The count of logged occasions stands at eleven and the count of occasions logged with no call in them stands at two, and neither is raised.** The bell answers in the road in the last paragraph of Chapter 80 and nobody writes it down and no count is made. Closes in Volume 15.
16. **Alden Marr. — CARRIED, untouched.** At the far bench in Chapter 80 and named once in Chapter 71 as the man the yard would not use to make a count of four read-backs come out right; not asked for anything, does not read Kest's statement aloud, and Calder Marr does not go. **No fragment, no report talk, no second story, and the repair pass did not put one in.** Closes in Volumes 9 and 15.
17. **The compartment, the dressed aperture, the custody numbers, the ten yards, the run under the town, the first echo's witness, Sera Mott. — CARRIED, all untouched.** No chapter of this batch refers to any of them. **No line is drawn between the three points and the ruling stands.** The run's four hundred feet stand as they stood on the nineteenth of January. **Sera Mott is not asked in this batch and may be asked once in this volume, in Movement 4.** Closes in Volumes 4, 5, 6, 9, 15 and across the series.

### Threads opened by Batch 0003

17. **A working boat in a lighter. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5, and the echo of it runs to Volume 7.** The *Sea Wren*, her gear, her pump and her papers, held from Saturday the fourth of March in daylight on a form, in an endorsement carrying five lines in the master's own words with his name against them, and the master's logbook in a naval file. **The vessel is not glass and the confiscation is not about glass, and the form says in the office's own type that it is not claimed to contain any material whatever, and it is about the yard's market wall.** A copy of a leaf of the yard's own book is in the master's coat. Neither can be got back.
18. **A record that is asked to be made single, and cannot be. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.** The circuit office wants the sheet, the eleven copies wherever held, and the whole book. **The yard will deliver the entry of any single day on the written asking of the crew whose day it is, unshortened, with its reason and its two columns entire, and nothing else, and it has named the four holders so that any man entitled to ask them may do so without coming to the yard.** Nobody can make the record single, and the reason is on a page in a shed.
19. **A man with a slate in the fish market. — NEW, open. Closes in Volumes 2–4, and it must not become an antagonist.** About thirty, not naval, not from a court, not from the circuit office that holds Mara Venn's file, working through the market on **Saturday the eighteenth of March** asking four men the same four questions: whether the woman who wrote the fair copy of the sheet lost her office in January and in what month, what she examined in October, where that thing is now, and whether she still comes to the fish dock on a Tuesday. **He was refused three answers and given one, because the fourth of January is on a wall, and he wrote down the refusals, and Mara Venn says that is a different and a better thing for him than a refusal.** **The date is the eighteenth and not two days: Piet Marrow heard of it in the market on the eighteenth and carried it up the hill for three days, and her four days of silence run from the eighteenth to the twenty-second.** **He is unnamed, is not referred to again, and no office is named behind him.**
20. **Neave Tarrant, of the third lay, nine winters, out since the twenty-eighth of February. — CARRIED from Batch 0002, opened, and NOT resolved.** Two boats of the herring fleet out on Saturday the eighteenth, Sunday the nineteenth and Monday the twentieth of March, one of them back with a man in her who was not looking for him; four third-lay boats doubled; a light on the mole head at dusk on the eighteenth and taken in on Monday the twentieth because the harbor authority's repair account is empty and there was no money in it for oil, with Neale's notice nailed to the tide-gang store door on the night of the eighteenth and the third lay dark from the night of the nineteenth; **a boy of fifteen refused a search by the shipwright and a man refused a search by himself, with the notice checked and the checking entered.** **There is not one line about him in the yard's book and the boy has entered that he looked twice. He is not found in this batch and is not to be found in Movement 4.** Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.
21. **A pump that cannot be hired, and a man who is out. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.** The fourth of March and the twenty-eighth of February are ten days apart and both are in the same book, and the boy has written them side by side without an opinion under them, and the yard is the first thing anybody in the harbor looks at when a man does not come back, and it has been the reason once already this month.
22. **A clerk who keeps a page. — PART, and the page needed two sides.** The harbor authority's clerk now has a reason with no purpose in it, a dive record in a boy's hand, a boy's answer in the margin, a second seal read in on a motion, **a naval count entered beside the yard's two dates, a ruling about inspections entered in a reeve's own words, a conservator's refusal entered at her own asking, a statement of the thirty-first entered in the same ledger, and a paper of the circuit office's date entered beside the date the yard was told.** On the fifteenth of March he says out loud that this morning was the first morning his page had needed two sides and that nobody told him to keep it and he is going to keep it. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 4 and after.
23. **A reeve who has spent everything. — NEW, PART. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.** Kest's own account, given out loud in a shed on the last day of a charter: four spendings since the twelfth of January, every one of which cost somebody who was not the yard a berth, a rope or a hearing; **no favour left to give and none coming; a second season out of a city that this office will read with everybody else; a count he sent up to the circuit office at his own office's cost because he would not be the only fool in a file in Veyr; and the word *suspend* said nine times in a room with a naval officer writing.** **He is not a friend and he is not to be softened. He becomes a man whose promises have dates on them, and the last date has passed.**
24. **The note nobody has re-written. — PAID, in the form the resolution allows.** The chandler offers a fortnight in his own hand for nothing; the yard refuses, in his own mouth, **at the head of its own dock, to the creditor who came down to it unasked rather than up to his shop**, and gives the reason: a yard that goes to its creditor with no date in its hand is a yard that has admitted it has nothing. **The three counts of the weeks are said out loud together for the first time: the creditor's twenty-two, the yard's thirteen, and eleven as last written, and neither man has moved his number since the fourth of January.** The note stands at twenty-seven. Closes as a figure in Volume 2 Movement 5 and the road after it is long.
25. **The Volume 2 midpoint. — NOT OPENED. Closes in Volume 2, Movement 4, Chapters 81–90.** No claim seal is proved forged and no coast road that no chart carries is described, checked or resolved in this batch. **A coast road is spoken of once, in a coaster's master's mouth, in the ordinary sense of the coasting trade and his Tuesday schedule, and it leads to nothing.** Movement 2's capacity figures are not fused with any road.

### Author-level decisions taken in this batch

- **The power rules are unchanged and no stage is taken.** Calder does not dive in this batch. **What the batch pays is one refusal that costs money** — a job on the last day of a charter turned away for want of an instrument — **and the repetition of the yard's own method in three directions it did not choose:** a ground of four paragraphs to an office, a leaf copied out of the yard's book for the man the yard has injured, and an offer to deliver any single day of the book to a circuit office on the written asking of the crew whose day it is. **The two-column convention is used twice more, on a day that a customer paid for and on a day the crew lost, and in both the reason for the column is given out loud.** **The tinnitus is NOT named in this batch.** Chapter 74 has the last sentence of the reason recited for the sixth time since the fourteenth of October and no naming of the condition; it was last named in Chapter 67, for the fifth time, and a later volume may name it once more. It has not vanished silently and it is not used here.
- **The consent rules are under load for the third time and hold.** Another crew's day is in the yard's book and now in a naval file and in a man's coat; **the yard will not ask a holder of a copy to give it up, and has named four holders in public instead, and says on the page that it has not asked and will not**; the yard gives a man the one document that concerns him and his crew and asks him for nothing; **Mara Venn asks to be named on a page rather than named at it and is asked nothing at all.** No seal, limit or refusal is overridden anywhere in the batch and no emergency access is used.
- **The mystery chain is unchanged and not advanced.** No chapter explains the Quietening, identifies the Crown Seal, names the Nine Harbors, explains the nine-line mark, proves who altered the manifest, reveals what the sealed case held, names the Office of Returning Memory, or puts a senior examiner in a room. **The run under the town is not touched.** **Captain Veyra Holt appears once, as a signature on a paper of the eighth of November already on a wall. Ilya Serr and Halven Rook do not appear anywhere in the batch, on paper or otherwise.**
- **The ending is untouched and no new antagonist, institution or final enemy appears.** The only new named people are ordinary working people already on the page or new to it in an ordinary way: **Amos Pryde's refusal is his own**, and the one wholly new person is **a man with a slate in a market, who is unnamed, is refused, is not referred to again, and is not an antagonist.** The **Volume 2 midpoint is not in this batch and the climax is not begun.**
- **A shape and length record, measured and not defended.** **26,358 words across ten chapters; eight inside the 2,200–3,200 guideline and two past it**, the largest Chapter 80 at about five and a half per cent over — the batch's one complete day and one room — and Chapter 76 at three per cent over, the batch's one hearing room. **No chapter in the batch is two rooms.** Chapter 80 was thirteen per cent over and carried the chandler's shop as a second room until the review repair moved the creditor down to the yard; the account is in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0003 review repairs*. The hearing room is used once, in Chapter 76, a fortnight after the hearing room of Chapter 70. The batch's pressures alternate: a decision and its price, a confiscation, a journey and a counter, a supply problem paid, an argument, a hearing, a market, labour and a missing man, a refusal, and a ledger.
- **A discipline, not a canon change.** The **three seals on the circuit office's file all carry nineteen words and the second and third differ only in their first clause**, and that is now on the page with a clerk saying that the line was in the office's type before the year turned. **The chandler's note carries three counts of its own weeks — twenty-two, thirteen, and eleven as last written — and none of them moves with the March fortnights, because each man is counting from his own start and neither will be moved.** A later volume may not advance any of the three by arithmetic.
- **One reconciliation, flagged rather than hidden.** **The circuit office's paper of the thirtieth of December and its second seal of the twenty-seventh of January both said ten copies, and the fourth request of the sixteenth of March says eleven.** The correction was made by the harbor authority, in the circuit office's own type, in a day, off a paper of Kest's forwarded on the fifteenth, and the yard learned of it from a form. **The question entered on the twenty-seventh of January — has the office been told, told and not corrected, or told and left standing — is therefore answered in the first half of Movement 3 and the answer is *told, by a third party, at a cost to that third party*.** The *Vigil's* slate still says ten and both figures stand in two ledgers with their dates, and neither is scratched out. **The count was never wrong. The count was corrected by somebody else.**

**Review artifact.** **Batch 0003 has been reviewed and repaired.** The reviewer pass over Chapters 71–80 returned **FIX**, and **every finding is disposed of in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0003 review repairs — the disposition of every finding*, which is the authority for that repair; the reviewer's own log is not kept in this repository and is not cited here.** Seven required chapter repairs, four state-file repairs, four prose repairs, and one unflagged defect of the same family found while applying them. **The repair altered no canon, no outline, no bible, no ending and no lock; it corrected dates, two figures, one false fact on a form, one piece of meta-fiction, four British spellings, a duplicated section break, and one chapter's staging, and it withdrew two false claims the state files had made about their own chapters.**

---

## Volume 02, Batch 0004 (Chapters 81–90) — as written, Movement 4, the forged seal. **THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT IS PAID.**

**Volume 02 is in progress. Chapters 81–90 are written and Movement 4 is complete. This block marks every thread the batch moved, every thread it opened, and every author-level decision it made. No thread below is closed by a chapter unless a chapter says so on the page.**

### Threads carried from Volume 01 and Batches 0001–0003 and moved here

1. **The circuit office's second request and the second seal. — CARRIED, unchanged, and named again.** The fourth request of the sixteenth of March **is still unanswered and unrefused at the end of this batch**, and the count of the copies of the public sheet in that office's own type is **eleven**, and the *Vigil's* slate still says ten as of the eighteenth of December, and both stand. **Kest's warning of the seventh of April is the batch's new pressure on it: the circuit office decides provenance and custody, it has wanted the yard's record since December, and a party with nothing in its hand cannot refuse in a way that reads like a refusal.** Closes across Volumes 2–4; the answer reaches Veyr with the writ in Movement 5.
2. **The public record as a thing other people hold. — CARRIED, and put to its hardest test in this batch.** The ninth thing on the market wall says the count is eleven and is eleven still, and that no holder has been asked for any of them and will not be, **and no holder is asked in this batch and nothing is delivered.** The record's own argument is answered in the market by Noll Tarner in the yard's own mouth — *a record of a dock is not for the people who keep the ground, it is for the people who come after you* — **which is the first time in six months that anybody has offered the yard an answer to Harl Dace's question of *who is it for*, and it is an answer about time and not about consent, and the volume may not treat it as the answer.** Closes nowhere; it runs to Volume 15.
3. **The count of the copies, out of date in two offices. — CARRIED, static, and now the yard's own currency.** Eleven on the yard's wall and in the circuit office's type, ten on a naval slate, both standing, and the yard has stated the count publicly twice in a month. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5 and the echo of it runs to Volume 7.
4. **The supply problem. — PAID as an income and PAID AGAIN as its absence.** The pump **went out once, on the fifth of April, at four marks, and stood idle for the eight days after that**, and it is the only gear in this harbor a man can hire that is not the harbor authority's, and there is now nobody with money who wants it in a blow, **and the batch names the consequence in a line: the yard could not sell the day it spent checking a bar of gravel.** Closes as an income in Volume 2 Movement 5 and the iron stays owed to the chandler.
5. **The rival crew, Noll Tarner. — CARRIED, and turned again, and he is still not a villain.** He reads the ninth thing on the market wall on the fifteenth and says the first true thing anybody has said on that wall about what a record is for, and he says it about the yard and not about himself, and he is not thanked. He and his crew are at the pile. Closes in Volumes 2–4.
6. **Elias Voke's statement of capacity. — CARRIED.** On its way to a coast office, read against the yard's own record on the fifteenth of March, and not referred to again in this batch. The coast office has not answered it and the hard at Ashtide is unanswered since the ninth of November. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.
7. **The charter as a leash. — PAID, and now an absence with a shape.** The charter ended at midnight on the thirty-first of March and **the yard's only instrument in this batch is a claim window for one object on one date**, which is not a charter, does not lead to one, and is granted by a reeve who says the whole of the yard's right to stand on that mole this month is a nicety it neither earned nor was given. **A job on the mole's toe is refused for want of an instrument and a job on the second seam is taken because no instrument is needed, and both answers are given in the same morning and the difference between them is the whole of what a harbor's paper is for.** Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5, where the provisional writ with a reporting clause is taken in its place.
8. **The hard at Ashtide. — CARRIED.** Unanswered since the ninth of November, and named once in this batch **in the provenance clause of a claim seal on a piece of worked glass**, which is not an answer and is not a connection. Closes in Volumes 2–3.
9. **The ninth seam's mooring, the north berth, the warp, the third lay, the mole-head light. — CARRIED, all unchanged, all named again on the seventeenth.** In service and a mooring and not a berth; the north berth out and the only wall that takes a north-easter; fifty fathoms of warp where ninety were to lie and forty feet of the authority's marline in the sea; **three tide-gang men off again from the first of April; the third lay full; the mole-head light not on and not to be, and the repair account empty since the middle of February; the *Kittiwake* on the hard with no cradle.** Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.
10. **A wage at four marks a week. — PART, and it has moved twice.** The **ninth week fell due on Tuesday the fourth of April and the tenth on Tuesday the eleventh, and both are entered unpaid in a column with no adjectives, so twenty-four marks are owed on the seventeenth of April and the eleventh falls on the eighteenth.** The boy has not asked and has not been told a date and nobody in the crew has asked him. **In this batch he is also told to be off the stone by the middle of May whatever his own arithmetic says, and the bonesetter's book still says a boat hand in June and June is eight weeks off and is not resolved.** Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5, where a boy is paid out of somebody else's pocket in part.
11. **The forty marks twice. — CARRIED, both whole, both unpaid, neither reduced, and neither paid in this batch.** On the twelfth of April **Mara Venn is refused four marks in writing by a shop on the quay at Ashtide for an opinion she gave in a market**, and enters the refusal and the figure in her own column and says out loud that the forty marks are not one mark smaller for it. **The yard's debt to Mara Venn is not paid and may not be paid in this volume.** Closes in Volume 4 at her break, and the debt's last payment is later.
12. **The bellwright's eight marks and the going out. — PART, and the cost has turned into neglect.** The eight marks are still owing and still cannot be found before October, **and there has not been a going out since the twenty-seventh of February, so the core has now gone four months without a boat and the rope on it is a year old in January**, and Chapter 90 ends the batch on the fact that nobody in that yard has said out loud what happens in June. **The bell does not sound in this batch and no occasion is entered and no count is made.** Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5 in a scene that pays the eight marks, and the craft closes in Volume 5.
13. **Mara Venn. — PART, and she is expensive and right and does not break.** She states four conditions before she touches anything. **She proves the claim seal forged in her own competence, without a diver and without a ship, and she says the hinge sentence — that she can say the seal was not struck by that office and cannot say who held the tool, who made the glass, or why a man wrote his own boat down as the thing that owns a record of the sea — out loud, before anybody asks it, and says she is not being careful but telling the exact size of what her trade can do.** **She refuses the asking for a figure for the third time in five months, identically in principle and newly in detail.** She will not let a false seal be described by a conservator in a market, she puts *unsigned* under her own finding, she refuses to witness nothing and refuses to be certified by a party, and she is refused four marks in writing. **Her break is Volume 4 and is not near.**
14. **The concealment. — CARRIED, hidden, and it is not touched in this batch.** The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation. **The false line is still false and the slate was not moved.** The boy has not asked and has not been asked, and on the ninth of April **he does not write a name he was asked not to write and was not asked about and did not ask** — the first line in the yard's book with a hole in it that is not a hole. And the holder comes within four inches of asking the conservator a fifth time and does not, and is told by a shipwright that he has asked four people nothing this month. Closes in Volume 9 and Volume 15.
15. **The delayed note. — CARRIED, untouched, and the count is protected explicitly.** **Eleven logged occasions, two empty answers, neither raised. The harbor's tide bell goes once in this batch, in the road, and no occasion is entered and no count is made, and the chapter says on the page that the count stands where it stood on the thirty-first of March because nothing has been added to it.** The answer inside the note is not approached, not named and not resolved. Closes in Volume 15.
16. **Alden Marr. — CARRIED, untouched.** At the far bench in Chapters 81, 82 and 90, not asked for anything, did not read the two columns, and Calder Marr does not go. **No fragment, no report talk, no second story, and Chapter 90 says so in its own words.** Closes in Volumes 9 and 15.
17. **The compartment, the dressed aperture, the custody numbers, the ten yards, the run under the town, the first echo's witness, Alden Marr. — CARRIED, all untouched.** **No chapter of this batch refers to any of them.** The claim window says on its face that the object is not one of the three objects entered in the circuit custody room on the fourteenth of October. **No line is drawn between the three points and the ruling stands.** The run's four hundred feet stand as they stood on the nineteenth of January. Closes in Volumes 4, 5, 6, 9 and 15.
18. **Sera Mott's half. — PART, and asked once and refused, in a private scene, and the volume may not ask again.** On Sunday the ninth of April Calder Marr asked her one question — whether there has ever been a man who went out in a boat to keep one of them and came back and told her what it was like — and she refused, and held to the December sentence that she will say no one more time in about two years. **Her name is not written in the yard's book at her asking, and the entry says she was asked and refused, and the boy did not write the name and was not asked to and did not ask.** She refuses, on the page and for the right reason, to have her half and the coast road in one book: **one is a road of water and the other is about men, and a page that holds both can be read either way, and it would be the first false thing anybody ever said about either of them.** Closes across the series; the first half of the answer is Volume 5 and the argument is Volume 11. **She may not be asked again in this volume.**

### Threads opened by Batch 0004

19. **A coast road that no current chart of this coast includes. — NEW, and the volume's midpoint. Closes across Volumes 2–5, and its echo runs to Volume 7's chart question.** Described by Harl Dace, master of the *Grey Widow*, of no crew of this association, forty-eight, thirty-one years in this market, given on his own account, not paid, not engaged, and not asked to check anything first: **three marks — the Old Custom House, a cairn of ballast stones eleven feet above a point, drying at half-tide; the Nine Foot, a sandspit with nine fathoms of a ship's stem on it called the Accountant, nine fathoms over it; and the Kettle, a rock that dries at low water springs three days out of a month — a bar of old gravel that comes up to two and a half fathoms in the middle of a spring, and the Rooks, a creek mouth that is the only shelter on five miles of that coast in a north-easter and is entered on the flood and never on the ebb.** **Part of it was checked on the ground in daylight with a boat and a hand lead on Thursday the thirteenth of April and the check cost two marks of victuals and a day the yard could not sell, and it found the cairn at eleven feet and the stem at nine fathoms and the bar at two and three-quarters on the flood off a spring, and did not look at the third mark because the Kettle was not drying and five miles of that coast was not looked at and cannot be.** **The harbor authority's own sounding sheet has no line inshore of the headland for five miles north, because there is nothing there to moor on and nobody to moor, so the check could only ever be a check of a man.** Two corroborations, both free and both in a market, one objecting and one confirming. **A later volume may not explain the road, may not name it as a network, may not connect it to anything under the town, and may not resolve it.**
20. **A piece of worked glass with a forged claim seal on it, in nobody's keeping. — NEW. Closes across Volumes 2–5.** A listening panel, worked in a bellwright's shop on the quay at Ashtide out of glass recovered, bought out of that shop by Harl Dace in the autumn, with Corrie Ash of the *Anna Beth*, a boat hand of working age laid up in the winter with his chest, as the witness. **The panel's age is not established: Mara Venn refuses on the page to give a figure for it, and the thirty-year dating and the wear argument that an earlier draft carried are withdrawn. No later volume may date the panel or restore that reading.** **Mara Venn proves the claim seal forged on two findings, both checkable by any man: the ring has nine joins in it with a ridge of lifted glass standing on the outside of every one of them running the same way round, where a Registry striking is cut once into steel and pressed down and not hit and has no ridge anywhere; and the custodian on the seal is a boat, and a boat cannot be asked anything.** The seal reads *Custodian, the sloop Grey Widow of Morrow Quay. Provenance, one panel worked at the quay of Ashtide. Limit, route use.* **The object is in Harl Dace's own hands, was examined under a claim window of Friday the seventh of April for one object on one date, and has never been in the yard's keeping for one hour.** **A later volume may not resolve who made the glass, may not name the shop, may not read any content out of the panel, and may not treat the panel as a chart, a key or a custody object.**
21. **A finding that is true and that no person with an office in this harbor may enter or sign. — NEW. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5, and the shape of it runs to Volume 4.** The conservator's finding of Monday the tenth of April is in the yard's own book in her words and her own hand, **with the word *unsigned* under it and the reason — no office in this harbor has entered it and the reason why is the fourth of January** — and a copy is in the harbor authority's clerk's own book, and the clerk said out loud on the seventeenth that the wall is a convenience and the ledger is the record. **The office cannot enter an examination of an object; it can enter that the object was on the ground on the date and that nobody went in the water.** Movement 5 must decide what a yard does with a true document it cannot certify.
22. **The fifth acknowledgement, and the first one a party asked for. — NEW, and `outline/volume-02.md` lock L24 worked in a new form. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.** The harbor authority's reeve entered, on the seventh of April and at the asking of this yard, that it has no power to lift the notice of the thirty-first, is not lifting it, and is putting a party, an object and a clerk inside a closure it did not create, **and is treating the whole of the mole from the third to the eleventh seams as inside the first clause for the purposes of that instrument whether the crown is inside it or not, and is treating it that way on purpose, because a conservative reading of a notice is not a narrow one.** A man in Veyr will now find five such entries in one ledger and the fifth is the only one a party asked for. **The cost of the acknowledgement is Kest's alone and it is still never free, and no later chapter of this volume may work ground the notice closes without it.**
23. **A claim given up on purpose, in a market, in front of the man who brought it. — NEW. Closes nowhere; it is the volume's argument.** The yard held the only claim it has ever had to a real object for four days and gave it back on the steps on the eleventh of April, **and the reason given in the shed was that it had been brought up the hill so that somebody with a trade could tell the man, and turning his question into the yard's asset while he stood in the room was not to be done.** **What the yard kept instead is two marks and a bar and a fisher's word on a wall with a nail in it, and the wall says in its own four lines that it is not a seal, is not a claim, is not a survey, binds nobody and consents for nobody.** This is the Movement 4 turn on the page and it is what a later volume may build on: *a false seal is worse held by a party than held by nobody.*
24. **The condition, named once more, and a good ear on a working deck. — PART. Closes in Volume 15.** The ringing, the late second sound, and the inability to place a sound in a boat are named on the *Grey Widow*'s after deck on the thirteenth of April, in the holder's own mouth, and put in a working man's own book so that it cannot be got back out of the yard's. **Harl Dace answers it as a working man — he has a man of fifty-one with it who is better on a lead and is not put on a lead, and he has not told him and does not intend to and would rather Calder Marr did not** — and Nessa Pike says the sentence to the wheel and not to him. **The condition is named for the sixth time since the fourteenth of October and may not be named again in this volume.**
25. **The chapter that is a refusal: the ninth thing on the market wall says that no person is to be asked for anything, and four men in that market would each have paid a mark for the line. — NEW. Closes in Volumes 5 and 9.** Amos Pryde's judgment — *a man who is hired out of a market for nineteen years has never had a thing said about him on a wall and would rather have that* — and the consent question's fourth form: **the record now has a line in it protecting its holders, published by the holders' creditor.** No Volume 2 chapter may answer what a record is for; the answer is the Open Keel Assembly's charter in Volume 9 and its public procedure in Volume 15.
26. **A man off a boat and a stove in the tide-gang store. — NEW, small, and it is the batch's pull. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.** On the seventeenth of April a coasting master asked whether this harbor had a room for a man off a boat, and **the honest answer was that the yard which owes a boy four marks a week and a bellwright eight marks will not put him in its shed**, and he went to the tide-gang store. **The weather in the batch has been falling since the third of April and has not made up into a rain, the third lay is full, and two boats were out on the Friday the third.** **Movement 5 owns the weather and everything that arrives with it.**

### Author-level decisions taken in this batch

- **The power rules are unchanged and no stage is taken.** Calder does not dive, does not enter anything, and uses nothing. **The condition is named once, on the page, in a working man's hearing, and a name is a stage being avoided by naming the thing that stops him working.** What the batch pays is method: a claim window asked for by name on one object and one date, a conservative reading of a notice entered by the reeve at the yard's asking, a reason in the yard's own book for a day that had a person in the water, with the diver named as not of the party, the limit his and fixed by him at the boat, the tender named and not the diver, and the cutoff hers to pull without asking anybody, and a second reason on the thirteenth with no cutoff invented for a man on a lead and the slate of limits carried for nothing, a two-column record of a thing the crew cannot check and a reason against every line of the second column, **and a refusal to hold a claim.**
- **The consent rules are under load for the fourth time and hold, and the batch adds a fifth form.** A claim seal on a piece of glass names a custodian, and the custodian is a boat, and **the volume's whole argument about custody is in that one clause and is proved on material and institutional grounds by a person with no standing.** A witness in the market is asked nothing and entered anyway. A man in a market is entered as an objector and entered again as unproved. A conservator is asked nothing and enters a finding on the record and refuses four marks for it. **A woman at the top of Coombe Row is asked one question, refuses, and her name is not written at her asking — and the yard enters the refusal instead, which is the first time this book has written down the absence of a fact as the fact.** No seal, limit or refusal is overridden anywhere in the batch and no emergency access is used.
- **The mystery chain is advanced by exactly one step and no more.** A road that no current chart includes exists and has been partly checked; a claim seal on this coast can be forged and competent; and the road is not explained, not named as a network, not connected to anything under the town, and not resolved. **The compartment is not referred to and the three-step finding is not restated. The dressed aperture and the fouling that gives no hour are not referred to. The nine-line mark is not explained and the Nine Harbors, the Quietening and the Crown Seal are not named. What was in the sealed case is not revealed, the manifest is not proved altered, the Office of Returning Memory is not named, and no senior examiner is in a room.**
- **The ending is untouched and no new antagonist, institution or final enemy appears.** The only new named people are ordinary working people: **Iven Toft, Harl Dace's account is his own and he is not new, Corrie Ash, Oswin Trask, Coll Ferrow, and a master of no crew of this association with a bad thumb who is not named.** **The clerk of the harbor authority is not an antagonist and becomes no more of one: he says what a box can hold, keeps a page, and says the wall is a convenience.** **Ilya Serr and Halven Rook appear nowhere in the batch, and Captain Veyra Holt does not appear on paper or otherwise.**
- **A shape and length record, measured and not defended.** **25,246 words across ten chapters; every chapter above the 2,200 floor and every chapter inside the 3,200 guideline** — the first Volume 02 batch to come in that way, and eleven to fourteen per cent under the ceiling on the two longest. **No chapter in the batch is two rooms.** The hearing room is not used at all; its slate wall is named twice, on the tenth and the seventeenth, and the clerk's own page is carried through both. The batch's pressures alternate: poverty and a refusal, paid work, a discovery, an office, a private refusal, material proof, an argument, action on the water, publication, and a ledger.
- **A discipline, not a canon change: the counts do not move with the calendar.** The three counts of the chandler's note stand at twenty-two, thirteen and eleven as last written and the note's mark figure moves only with bread. The count of the copies of the public sheet stands at eleven. The count of logged occasions in the delayed note stands at eleven and the two empty answers stand at two, and **Chapter 83 says on the page that the count stands where it stood on the thirty-first of March because nothing has been added to it.** No later volume may advance any of them by arithmetic.
- **One practice added, and it is a practice and not an instrument.** The harbor's eighth and the share are taken in April with no charter at all, on a trade argument entered in the first column with the man who argued it named against it. **This binds the yard's later arithmetic and changes no instrument.**
- **The volume's climax is not begun and nothing in this batch may be used for it.** No fishing crew is trapped, there is no glass storm, and no equipment is destroyed. The batch's pull is weather and a boat and a man asking for a stove.

**Review artifact.** **Batch 0004 has been reviewed and repaired.** The reviewer pass over Chapters 81–90, the four state files and the queued `batch-0005/PROMPT.md` returned **FIX**; **the reviewer's own log is not kept in this repository and the old citation is withdrawn**, and **every finding is disposed of in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0004 review repairs — the disposition of every finding*.** Five required repairs and ten prose, precision and file-reference repairs, and one unflagged defect of the same family found while applying them. **The repair altered no canon, no outline, no bible, no ending and no lock; it corrected a record that contradicted its own scene, withdrew a dating that could not be true, fixed a calendar shifted four days in the queued prompt, separated two purchases of air that were buying one pot, and settled one holder and one price for a copy of the ninth thing, and it corrected a broken sentence, two dates, one injury, two word-count figures, a column depth, a duplicated bad thumb, a counted weather series, a tense, a pair of mixed units and a dangling log reference.**

**Two of the five required repairs change what a later writer inherits and are marked on the threads above.** **Thread 20: the panel carries no date at all, and no later volume may give it one or restore the thirty-year reading.** **The fifth of April was a dive day, and the yard's reason for it names a diver who was not of the party, a limit he set himself at the boat, a named tender who was not the diver, and a cutoff she could pull without asking anybody — so a person in the water and a person on a lead are two different kinds of day in this yard's practice, and Movement 5 inherits the difference instead of a contradiction.** The volume's midpoint argument, the forged seal, the unsigned finding, the coast road, the claim given up on purpose and the man off a boat asking for a stove are all untouched by the repair.
