## Volume 04, Batch 0001 (Chapters 151–160) — as written, Movement 1, the notice and the seat. **THE BATCH MIDPOINT AND THE BATCH CLIMAX ARE PAID.**

**31,731 words across ten chapters as re-measured after this batch's review repairs, and 31,019 before them, Wednesday the sixteenth of August to Sunday the tenth of September 2026. Movement 1 is complete, the turn has landed, and the volume's central pressure is on the page. The full accounting is `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0001 as written (Chapters 151–160), Volume 04*, and the repairs are `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0001 (Volume 04) review repairs*, which is the authority for them. The six open threads are the six open threads and this batch opened no seventh. NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO SCENE WAS CUT, NO COST WAS DROPPED AND NO PLANNED PLOT WAS ALTERED.**

### Carried and moved

- **CARRIED, AND ADVANCED — Mara Venn.** She is in one chapter of this batch, on Friday the eighth of September, in a room she went into herself, and this party was on the steps outside for eighty minutes and did not go in. She is asked about five bells by a man in a coat and gives **six things and no seventh, and the seventh is six marks and two days**, and the two sixes are the same figure put down twice on purpose and she says so in the room. **THE SHARD OF WORKED GLASS NOBODY IN THIS HARBOR CAN READ STAYS UNREAD AND THE SIX MARKS AND TWO DAYS STAY UNPAID AND THE YARD DOES NOT OFFER THEM AND IS NOT ASKED TO AND HAS ENTERED THAT IT IS GOING TO SAY NO.** **Her forty marks stand unpaid, first after food, since the fifteenth of October, and are not reduced.** The not-asking is entered in the first column with a date and a reason, and a request to scrape that line out of the column is refused in the same words it is refused for a line in any column. **THE SENTENCE SHE TOLD HIM ON THE SEVENTH OF AUGUST NOT TO WRITE DOWN IS NOW WRITTEN DOWN, six weeks later, in the second column, with a reason against it and no name in it, and he has not told her he wrote it.** Her break with the Registry is not in this batch and no chapter of it fuses the two, and she did not come back to this crew and is not on it.
- **CARRIED, AND ADVANCED — the third column, the boy and the pencil.** **The third column was NOT filled on Sunday the tenth of September, which is the second day since the second of May that it has not been filled on a day, and the first column says why, and says again that this party does not know who will fill it next and has not appointed anybody and is not going to, and that a column nobody fills is a column somebody else fills.** **He is in no chapter of this batch as a person.** Nobody wrote to him, nobody went to see him, nobody asked anybody who has seen him what he said, and the three nots of the eighth of June stand. The head of the column keeps his hand and a request to scrape it out is refused. Forty marks stand whole, unpaid, not reduced, not asked to be reduced. **And the yard has entered that it is not going to hire a boy to read a position back at four marks a week and call that the same thing.** The person holding the pencil is Nessa Pike and she is not going to be thanked for it and neither is the holder.
- **CARRIED, AND ADVANCED — the nine-line mark.** Not referred to, not named and not explained in this batch. **It closes in Volume 5 and not before, and the three places with no joint are a different thing and were not asked about and were not converted into either.**
- **CARRIED, AND ADVANCED — the question about who changed the rule.** Asked in a market in this harbor on Sunday the thirteenth of August and not answered. **THE NOTICE OF THE TENTH OF SEPTEMBER DOES NOT ANSWER IT AND SAYS ON THE PAGE THAT IT DOES NOT, and the yard has entered in the first column that a man reading that notice in five years will not find in it one word about who changed a rule or what a rule was called from.** The not-answering stands and this party is not going to put a name on a thing it cannot check.
- **CARRIED, AND PAID TWICE — the second season of the writ, the first of March, the first of September, the unpaid wage and the three marks owing.** The second season is a named absence and **BOTH the form of the nineteenth of August and the notice of the tenth of September say on their face that they do not touch, grant, refuse or deal with it, and the yard has entered both halves: a paper that does not mention a thing is not a decision about the thing.** The writ runs to midnight on the first of March, is not the Second Bell license, does not lead to one, is not a charter, renews nothing, its ground is not widened and its four names are four. **THE NINTH SEAM'S MOORING'S END OF IT WAS THE FIRST OF SEPTEMBER AND IT IS BACK IN SERVICE, found sound by two men who read the date off a wall and were paid nothing by anybody.** **A WAGE OF FOUR WAS PAID ON TUESDAY THE TWENTY-SECOND AND FOUR ON TUESDAY THE TWENTY-NINTH, and the column stands at eighteen lines and seventy-two marks with the fifth of March the oldest, and THIRTEEN ENVELOPES ARE ON A BENCH AND ONE WEEK FALLEN DUE THIS YEAR HAS NO ENVELOPE STANDING AGAINST IT, being the week of Tuesday the fifteenth of August.** **THREE MARKS ARE STILL OWING TO A MAN OF ASHTIDE AND NOT A SHILLING OF IT HAS BEEN PAID**, and the reason is in the first column with the figure and the date, and he was named once in this volume and is not to be named again.
- **CARRIED, AND UNTOUCHED — the five bells at about five and a half fathoms inside the four hundred yards.** Not raised, not offered, not argued, not promised. The fact is on the market wall of the twenty-fifth of August in a block of its own, saying where they are, that they are not on the sheet and not for sale, and that they went back into the water out of a man's hands on the eighth of August. **The four people of the Sill hold a limit that stands, the holder is not going to ask them to take it off, and they were not asked anything in this batch.**

### Opened or advanced by this batch

- **NEW, AND THE VOLUME'S CENTRAL PRESSURE, NOW ON THE PAGE — THE INQUIRY'S NOTICE AND ITS CONSTRUCTION.** A four-paragraph notice served in daylight at the top of this mole's steps in about nine people's hearing on Sunday the tenth of September, appointing a sitting for **a day in the week following the tenth** at the hearing room over the tide-gang store at ten in the morning, and giving the harbor authority power to seat one of two disputants and take such finding as is given without prejudice to any claim. **That construction is the reporting clause's own second limb, applied to this party, and the party wrote the clause in April and cannot un-write it. The party's own sentence about it, in the shipwright's mouth in a shed at ten at night, is that the same box has been turned round so that we are the thing on the table and not the person reading the table.**
- **NEW, AND THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT IS APPROACHING AND IS NOT SPENT — A COPY OF THE YARD'S WHOLE CREW'S BOOK IS IN A FILE IN A CITY.** Fifty-one weeks, produced whole and unshortened in about ten minutes on Saturday the ninth of September, having said out loud before it was opened why it was being opened, **read in that room not one line**. A copy went into a file unopened and a man in a coat said at the table that he is not going to open it there and is going to read it in a city. **THE READING OF THAT BOOK IN A ROOM IS THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT AND IS IN CHAPTERS 181 AND LATER. NO CHAPTER OF MOVEMENTS 1, 2 OR 3 MAY READ A LINE OF IT ALOUD IN A ROOM.**
- **NEW, AND OWED — A PROVISIONAL PUBLIC COPY RECORD EXISTS AND HAS NOT BEEN GIVEN.** A copy of a record about an object, with the limits the record itself carries printed on the face of the copy. The yard may ask for a copy of the copy at its own cost; the request is entered; the copy is not made and is not released and no date attaches to either; and the yard may not have the object. **IT IS NOT OBTAINED, NOT MADE, NOT RELEASED, NOT PRINTED, NOT QUOTED AND NOT DESCRIBED BEYOND WHAT THE MAN IN A COAT SAID IN A MARKET OFFICE ON THE TWENTY-FIFTH OF AUGUST. ITS RELEASE IS THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX AND IS RESERVED TO MOVEMENT 5.**
- **NEW, AND OWED — THE YARD'S OWN LETTER OF THE TWENTIETH OF AUGUST AND ITS REFUSAL.** The yard asked a circuit office in a city, in the ordinary post, for a copy of the record of its own fourth return of the fourteenth of July, and has not been given it, and has published the asking and the not-being-given on a market wall with a date on it. **A request is a thing a party can publish. An answer is not, and the sheet says in its own words that this yard is not printing an answer it does not have. A copy of a record is not a thing anybody in this harbor can check.**
- **NEW, AND FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — THE FOURTH PARAGRAPH OF THE NOTICE.** The demand for any sheet or paper bearing this party's own figures, made by the party and put up on a public wall in this harbor on or about the twenty-fifth of August, with a copy of it. **Whoever drafted that notice has read a market wall of this harbor or has had somebody read one to him, and the yard does not know who and has entered that it does not know and is not going to guess.**
- **NEW, AND FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — THE CLERK READ PART OF THE NOTICE FROM MEMORY AND THEN SAID HE READ IT FROM THE PAPER.** Ambrose Sell read the first paragraph off the paper in his hand in the open air and the other three from memory, and then said that he had said it from the paper, and the yard has written down that he did not and that he said he did. **No Volume 04 chapter may improve on either.**
- **NEW, AND FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — THE SIXTH BUILDER'S FIGURE, SAID WRONG TWICE IN A MARKET.** Two hundred and seventy-seven feet for two hundred and seventy-nine, on the second reading, in about forty people's hearing, corrected off the sheet in the holder's own hand without going back to the top, in about a second and a half, with nobody saying anything, and entered in the second column against himself with a reason and **without a name of any kind in it.** **This is the only entry in the second column in five months that is a mistake of this yard's own rather than a limit of a man in a boat.**
- **NEW, AND FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — THREE SHEETS OFF A MARKET WALL IN ONE MORNING AND INTO A BOAT,** being the *Vigil's* notice of the twenty-eighth of July, the longer notice of the fourth of August, and this yard's own sheet of the fifteenth of August, with about nine of the forty copies on the same deck and the copy in the city at a press which cannot be stopped; **and three further sheets gone off that wall inside about nine hours of the same two days, on a day the yard does not know and has not asked about and is not going to guess.** The agent is a man in a coat in a boat, seen by a man of the third lay at about seven in the morning who gave his name and the hour and a quarter of an hour instead of a minute because he had no watch, and a barge out of the naval pile two hours out. **THE YARD WILL NOT NAME HIM IN A ROOM AND HAS NOT NAMED HIM IN A BOOK AND THE CHANNEL IS THE WRIT AND THIS IS NOT ABOUT THE SECOND SEASON.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — THE NINTH ACKNOWLEDGEMENT, AND A MAN OF THE FIRST LAY.** Entered on Monday the twenty-eighth of August in the reeve's own hand at his own cost on a day of work inside the first clause of the notice of the thirty-first of October, for a woman of the second lay with gear of her own under the mole's toe, at twelve of seventeen paid at the mole, the yard's own five out of the same board. **THE PRICE FELL ON CORBY VANCE OF THE FIRST LAY, WHO HAD THE TOE BOOKED FOR THE SAME MONDAY AND IS OUT OF A DAY AND A DAY'S WORK, and the yard entered his name and the figure and has not paid it, is not going to thank him, is not going to send for him, and is not going to say sorry in a market, and the reason is on the page.** **The ninth is the last and the tenth may not be entered, and the reeve has said out loud that this office has been asked twice by this party, has paid twice, and cannot pay a third time.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A DAY INSIDE THE FIRST CLAUSE, AND NO OTHER.** Monday the twenty-eighth of August, about four hours and ten minutes, in and out at the hour entered. **No person, no boat and no lead of this yard was inside the first clause of the notice of the thirty-first on any other day of this batch, and the eighth-clause plan of the seventeenth of January was not reopened, and the plate at the fifth seam was not reached, not measured, not touched and not asked about.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A WALL THAT TELLS A MAN WHERE THE COPIES ARE.** Rowan Trewin said it out loud on the mole's steps on Wednesday the twenty-third and the yard heard him and did not agree and said so on the sheet's own face on Friday the twenty-fifth, in about forty people's hearing, and is not taking it down. **A road nobody else can use is not a road and a road everybody can use is not this yard's, and both halves are on a market wall and neither is going away, and the two figures for the bar of gravel stand side by side in nineteen words and neither is withdrawn and this volume does not decide which is right.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A THIRD COLUMN NOT FILLED, AND A QUESTION THE YARD CANNOT ANSWER.** Whether the holder is going to read the second column out in a room, entered in the second column on the tenth with a reason against it and no name in it, and the reason that a man in a boat can check which of his own hands was on a line and a man in a room can be checked and this one prefers to be checked and does not yet know what he will say. **Three weeks. The yard has entered that it is not going to spend them reading anything.**
- **NEW, AND STANDING — THE READ-OUT DEVICE IS SPENT FOR THIS BATCH AND THE TWO-COLUMN HEAD-OF-DOCK READ-OUT IS NOT USED IN IT AT ALL — AND THE READ-BACK IS NOT THE READ-OUT, WHICH A REVIEWER PASS HAD TO CORRECT IN THIS FILE.** The read-out — a man reading figures out of the yard's own book aloud to people who can check them — happened once, on the twenty-fifth of August, at a market wall, of a **sheet** and not of the book, and the holder got a figure wrong the second time and corrected himself off the sheet in his hand. **A later volume and a later batch may use the read-out once; this batch has had its one. THE READ-BACK IN THE SHED — THE SHIP'S HAND READING AN ENTRY BACK AND THE HOLDER READING IT BACK AFTER HER, NOBODY ELSE IN THE SHED, AT NINE AT NIGHT — IS THIS YARD'S STANDING PRACTICE AND NOT A USE OF THE DEVICE: it runs in eight of the ten chapters — 151, 152, 153, 155, 156, 158, 159 and 160 — and Chapter 157 opens with a read-out of the yard's own book in a shed, and a later batch may use it on any night it likes without spending anything.**

### Ruled

- **RULED — THE CONDITION IS NAMED NOWHERE IN THIS BATCH.** The batch was permitted at most one naming and only if a chapter earned it, and no chapter earned it. **THE BARE FACT IS CARRIED TWICE IN THE SECOND COLUMN, BOTH TIMES WITH A REASON AGAINST THE LINE AND WITHOUT A NAME:** the not-reading of the crew's book in a hearing room on the ninth, and the not-knowing about reading the second column out in a room on the tenth. **The four lines above the named line of the twentieth of July are still four, are still nameless, are not struck out, and a request to strike one out of a column is refused in the same words for any column. The count of four stands in the first column in a ship's hand with no name against it.**
- **RULED — NO POWER STAGE OF ANY KIND WAS TAKEN AND NONE MAY BE IN MOVEMENT 1.** **A search of the ten chapters for *faultreader, bellwright stage, keelwright, storm-reader, chorus diver, openhand, tide-ear* returns nothing.** He reads no seam in this batch, on a document or on anything else, and no seam settles anything, proves anything, convicts anybody or tells him what happened. **He goes in no water in this batch.** The Bellwright stage is Volume 5 and requires a crew and Volume 04 may not take it.
- **RULED — THE BELL IS NOT AN INSTRUMENT AND WAS NOT TOUCHED.** No boat went off the south end in this batch, no second eight marks was entered as paid, the phrase *a tongue half worn through* does not occur in any of the ten chapters, the bellwright was not asked and is in no chapter of it as a person, the chest was not opened and was not brought down the hill, and **the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed and is carried in the close of Chapter 160.**
- **RULED — THE CUSTODY ITEMS ARE UNTOUCHED AND THE LOCK IS SATISFIED BY ABSENCE.** Three items, three numbers, **no digit quoted, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request, and the word *custody* does not occur once in the ten chapters of this batch.**
- **RULED — NO RETURN WAS MADE AND THERE IS NO FIFTH.** A finding of worked glass was made on no day of this batch and nothing was raised on any day of it, and the standing line for the eighth and ninth of August of Volume 03 is unchanged.
- **RULED — THE MAN IN A COAT IS NOT NAMED AND MAY NOT BE NAMED IN THIS VOLUME.** He is in a market office on the twenty-fifth of August and in a room on the eighth of September and on a mole's steps on the tenth, in the last of which he served a notice and did not take his coat off. **A search of the ten chapters returns nothing for *Trewhella*.** He is a channel and not a face, and no chapter of this batch gave him a coat off, an office, a name or an opinion about this yard.
- **RULED — AMBROSE SELL IS NAMED WHEREVER A CLERK OF THE CIRCUIT OFFICE APPEARS**, in Chapters 152, 159 and 160, and the harbor authority's clerk is not a man of any consequence and keeps his own page.
- **RULED — THE SENTENCE *BOTH OF THOSE ARE TRUE AND NEITHER OF THEM CANCELS THE OTHER* IS SAID ONCE IN THIS BATCH**, by the shipwright, in a shed, on Sunday the tenth, about the party's own clause being turned round. **No character remarks on how often it has been said, no character claims to have invented it, and no character remarks that it belongs to a Volume 02 room.**
- **RULED — THE SLATE WALL'S COUNTER DID NOT RESTART AND IS SIXTEEN, SEVENTEEN, EIGHTEEN, NINETEEN** on the twenty-first and twenty-eighth of August and the fourth and eleventh of September, all bare, all with the clerk keeping his own page, and no sitting held on any of them.
- **RULED — THE MARKET WALL AND THE HEARING ROOM ARE NEVER THE SAME DAY, VERIFIED TWICE.** The market wall is published on the sixteenth and the twenty-fifth of August; the hearing room is used on the ninth of September; the market office is used on the twenty-fifth of August and the eighth of September; no two of the three rooms share a day.
- **RULED — A CARD'S WEEKDAY WAS CORRECTED AGAINST ITSELF AND THE CHAPTERS ARE RIGHT.** `outline/batches/volume-04-batch-0001.md`'s Chapter 160 pull says a wage is due on Tuesday the fifteenth; **the fifteenth of September is a Friday on this book's grid and the next wage Tuesday is the nineteenth, and Chapter 160 says the nineteenth.**

### Open by design

- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the answer inside the delayed note.** Eleven logged occasions and two of them with no call in them, **neither advanced in this batch by arithmetic or by a measurement, no occasion entered, no measurement taken**, the delay a hand's width of a second and unmoved, and nobody in this yard and nobody in this harbor has said why. **The answer is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the release of the provisional public copy record.** It exists; the yard may ask for a copy of the copy at its own cost; the request is entered; the copy is not made and not released and no date attaches to either. **Its release is the volume's climax and is Movement 5.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the reading of the crew's book in a room.** The copy is in a file. The reading is the volume's midpoint and is Chapters 181 and later. **Nothing in this batch set it up and nothing in this batch paid for it beyond the copy.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — who drafted the fourth paragraph of the notice.** It requires a sheet of this party's own figures put up on a public wall on the twenty-fifth of August, with a copy. **Somebody has read a market wall of this harbor. The yard does not know who and is not going to guess.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN, AND RESTORED TO WHERE THE OUTLINE KEEPS IT — the page gone out of a file. IT WAS SPENT IN MOVEMENT 1 BY A REVIEWER PASS AND HAS BEEN TAKEN BACK OUT.** Chapter 156 asserted as a fact that there is a page gone out of an office's file and that the plate note closes in this month with a document; **that is the volume's resolution element and Movement 3's turn, it is reserved to the volume's close by `outline/volume-04.md` § *Midpoint reversal* and § *Concrete resolution*, and no chapter before 171 may produce, quote, summarize or describe a removal, including saying that one happened.** **THE PLATE NOTE IS OPEN AND SAYS SO: the plate is in eleven feet, seen and not measured and not touched, the eight-clause plan of the seventeenth of January was refused in a record with a date on it, this party has not written a better letter and is not going to, and whatever closes that note will close it in an office and not in a shed, and this party does not know what that is and has entered that it does not know.** Alden's silence is unaffected. **No confession, no fragment, no report talk, and no removed page on any page of this batch.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the three places with no joint.** Carried on the page of the twenty-fifth of August in the yard's own words: this yard does not know why the three are there and is not going to guess. **They are owed a placement in a later volume as a fact and not as an identification, and they are not the nine-line mark and may not be fused with it.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — what the shard, the packing and the marking on the mole's crown carry.** Not read, not identified, not settled. The two objects of nobody's have not moved and one tarpaulin still has a corner off it since the seventeenth of June.
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the coast road.** Described, published, doubted, priced in a city, and **not resolved and not called anything.** Five miles of it not looked at, and the yard has entered on the page that it has not looked and is not going to in the season and that this is a working reason and not a virtue.
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the custody items and the live question no paper in this harbor answers**, and the lead plug's number, and the run under this town, and the compartment, and the first echo's witness, and Neave Tarrant, nine winters, out since the twenty-eighth of February, not found, one line, and this yard has not gone.

### What Movement 2 inherits, in one place

1. **A date and a room, and three weeks.** A sitting on a day in the week following Monday the eleventh of September, at the hearing room over the tide-gang store at ten in the morning. **The hearing room and the market wall are never the same day, and this yard is going to have to be in a room in one week and on a wall in another.**
2. **The crew's book is in a city in a copy and the reading of it in a room is the volume's midpoint**, and no chapter of Movement 2 may read a line of it aloud in a room, produce a copy of a record in a room, or put a seam on a document.
3. **A bag with a mark and twelve shillings and fourpence in it, three marks owing to a man of Ashtide unmoved since the eighth of August, seven pence owing to this yard's own shipwright for the postage of a letter of the twentieth of August and not paid, a wage of four due on Tuesday the nineteenth, a day at the second seam's own face that has brought five and five this month, the compressor's gland unpaid and useless, the note at thirty-one and not re-written, and nothing in a tin since the twenty-eighth of April.** No office in this harbor has paid this yard a mark since the eighth of May.
4. **A ninth acknowledgement and no tenth, and the reeve has nothing left to put in that ledger**, and the price of the ninth fell on a man of the first lay and is not going to be paid and is not going to be thanked for.
5. **A provisional public copy record that exists and has not been given**, and a yard that has published a request with a date on it and a refusal with no date on it, and has said in its own hand that it is not printing an answer it does not have.
6. **A market wall carrying a sheet of the twenty-fifth of August over this yard's own fourth return**, and a return that is still the only paper on that wall any office in any city is obliged to look at, and a road that nobody else can use and a road everybody can use standing side by side on it in nineteen words.
7. **A conservator of no office who is in this harbor on Tuesday and Friday mornings because that room is open to anybody, who was asked six questions in one of them on the eighth of September, who is owed forty marks first after food, and whose break with the Registry is this volume and a different event from her leaving this crew, and who did not come back to this crew and is not on it.**
8. **A second column with three new lines in it, none of them named, and seventeen first-column entries in thirty-eight paragraphs and six second-column entries across the ten chapters of Movement 1, which is the inventory the next volume inherits and can recount (two of the six second-column entries do not open with the words *Second column*, so the method matters),** and a first column with an entry in it that says a man in a boat lost the count of his own basket and a man in a market got a figure of a builder's day-book wrong in front of forty people, and a request to strike one out of either column is refused in the same words for any column.
9. **A harbor that is poorer on the tenth of September than it was on the fifteenth of August**, by one day of a man's work and by nothing that anybody did for it: one herring berth out, the north berth out, a light not on, a cistern on a rota, a tide gang four short, a fire party's hose dead, a hard at Ashtide unanswered since the ninth of November, nineteen winters in a list in a city, and the *Sea Wren* at the pile with this party not having thanked him.
10. **And the six open threads, unchanged in number.** Mara Venn's break with the Registry, which is this volume and is not her leaving this crew; the third column, the boy and the pencil; the nine-line mark, which closes in Volume 5; the question about who changed the rule; the second season of the writ and the first of March and the wage and the three marks; and the five bells at five and a half fathoms with the four people of the Sill who have no boat.

### Thread hygiene for the Volume 04 writer

**Six open threads. No seventh. ## Volume 04, Batch 0002 (Chapters 161–170) — as written, Movement 2, the work that cannot be sold while a book is being read

**29,302 words across ten chapters as re-measured by the Volume 04 close audit after its nineteen mechanical repairs, in place of the 29,289 this section gave, which is withdrawn and named as withdrawn here rather than silently replaced; that 29,289 was itself the figure after this batch's own review repairs, and 28,941 was the figure before them. Monday the eleventh to Saturday the thirtieth of September 2026. Movement 2 is complete, the turn has landed, and the four words in a file in a city are on the page. The full accounting is `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0002 as written (Chapters 161–170), Volume 04*, and the repairs are `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0002 (Volume 04) review repairs*, which is the authority for them. The six open threads are the six open threads and this batch opened no seventh. NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO SCENE WAS CUT, NO COST WAS DROPPED AND NO PLANNED PLOT WAS ALTERED.**

### Carried and moved

- **CARRIED, AND ADVANCED — Mara Venn. This is the movement's second half and the volume's relationship spine.** She is asked for the first time since the twenty-third of July, on Friday the twenty-ninth of September, in a room open to anybody, in eleven people's hearing, and she prices the ask before she answers it. **THE PRICE IS NOT SIX MARKS AND TWO DAYS AND IS NOT MONEY: it is an undertaking, in this yard's own first column, in the holder's own left hand, with a date on it, that this party does not ask her for anything again — not at a figure, not at nothing, not for a line, not for a name, not in a room, not in a shed, not in a market, and not in about two years when he will have had a bad month.** The reason is her own sentence of the seventh of August, which he worked out in the first week of August and has not had to say out loud since and she has. **THE ANSWER IS NO, and it is no at a figure and no at nothing, and no money changed hands and no sheet was asked for at the press and the slab was not shifted.** He says he is not going to thank her and is not going to say he is sorry. **HER FORTY MARKS STAND UNPAID, FIRST AFTER FOOD, SINCE THE FIFTEENTH OF OCTOBER, AND ARE NOT REDUCED, and the six marks and two days are unpaid, unoffered and are not going to be.** **HE STILL HAS NOT TOLD HER HE WROTE THE SENTENCE DOWN IN THE SECOND COLUMN ON THE EIGHTH AND THE NOT-TELLING STANDS.** **HER BREAK WITH THE REGISTRY IS NOT IN THIS BATCH, IS A DIFFERENT EVENT FROM HER LEAVING THIS CREW, IS MOVEMENT 3'S BEAT, AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH SHOWS IT, HINTS AT IT, OR HAS A CHARACTER REMARK THAT SHE IS NOT WHERE SHE WAS. She did not come back to this crew and is not on it.**
- **CARRIED, AND ADVANCED — the third column, the boy and the pencil.** **The third column was not filled on any day of this batch and a first-column line says the party does not know who will fill it next and has not appointed anybody and is not going to appoint anybody in a shed on a Monday night, and that a column nobody fills is a column somebody else fills. And on Sunday the seventeenth the shipwright says out loud, in the first column, that she did not fill it, that she thought about it for about a minute and a half, that she is the only one in that yard who could have done it with two strokes and nobody would ever have known, and that a column kept by a shipwright in a shed on a Sunday is a column about a shipwright.** He is in no chapter of this batch as a person except as a fore berth in June in the mouth of **Denzil Hare**, of the third lay, who came up the hill in the rain and was not asked one question. **The three nots of the eighth of June stand. Nobody wrote to him, nobody went to see him, nobody asked anybody who has seen him what he said. The head of the third column keeps his hand. Forty marks stand whole.**
- **CARRIED, AND ADVANCED — the nine-line mark.** Not referred to, not named, not explained, and not asked about in this batch. **It closes in Volume 5 and not before, and the three places with no joint are a different thing and were not converted into either.**
- **CARRIED, AND ADVANCED — the question about who changed the rule.** Asked in a market in this harbor on Sunday the thirteenth of August and not answered. **NOTHING IN THIS BATCH ANSWERS IT AND NOTHING IN THIS BATCH ASKS IT AGAIN, and a first-column entry on the Wednesday the twenty-seventh puts the position on the page: a wall can be asked a question of and a refusal cannot be corrected by anybody who is not its author, and both of those are true, and the party does not know which is going to cost more in October.**
- **CARRIED, AND PAID TWICE — the second season of the writ, the first of March, the unpaid wages and the three marks.** The second season is a named absence and **nothing in this batch touches, grants, refuses or deals with it**, and the direction of the sixteenth of September says so on its own face. **On Tuesday the twelfth the yard records in the first column that it did not go to the harbor authority and did not ask that office for anything, and why: the ninth is the last entry in that ledger, the officer said out loud on the twenty-seventh of August that his office has been asked twice and has paid twice and cannot pay a third time, and a yard that has been told that does not go back up the same steps with the same request. It is entered as a decision and not as a virtue and nobody is going to thank this party for it.** **THREE MARKS ARE STILL OWING TO A MAN OF ASHTIDE AND NOT A SHILLING OF IT HAS BEEN PAID, he was named once in this volume and is not named again, and no chapter of this batch asks him a question.**
- **CARRIED, AND UNTOUCHED — the five bells at about five and a half fathoms inside the four hundred yards.** Not raised, not offered, not argued, not promised, and not mentioned as anything anybody can do. The four people of the Sill hold a limit that stands, the holder did not ask them to take it off, and they were not asked anything in this batch; a second-column line on the Sunday the twenty-fourth records that they have about nine marks between them and no boat, that they have not asked this party for anything, and that this party would like to and has had nothing.

### Opened or advanced by this batch

- **NEW, AND THE MOVEMENT'S TURN, AND IT IS PAID — A REFUSAL TO CERTIFY THAT HAS BEEN READ IN A CITY AS A CLAIM.** Asked on Monday the twenty-fifth by an ordinary working man with a paper of his own, refused in a doorway in about eleven sentences **for four working reasons** — a certification is a conclusion with a name under it, the figure was made once in a quarter of an hour, there is a man of fifty-four whose figure is in the same press and it is not the yard's to decide between them, and a printed thing cannot be certified afterwards without becoming a different kind of thing. **On Wednesday the twenty-seventh the yard learns that a surveyor in a city has entered, as her own finding, that the yard declines to certify the figure and will not be held to it, and the four words *will not be held to it* were never said by this party, and she asks in her own letter whether they are the yard's own words and gives her reason for entering them.** **And the reason she entered the refusal at all, on the page, in her own hand, in a report written before the refusal was made: she had applied in writing and had no answer, and she took it from a man of the first lay who crossed the water, and she wrote on the face of the entry that she had not had it from the yard.**
- **NEW, AND THE VOLUME'S FORWARD PULL — THE ONLY WAY TO BE READ CORRECTLY IS A NAME, AND THE ONLY PERSON ON THIS COAST WHO CAN PUT A TRADE AT THE FOOT OF A LINE IS NOT ON THIS CREW.** Said out loud in a doorway on the twenty-seventh by a man of fifty-one: a man with a page cannot act on a refusal, he can act on a name, and there are three people on this coast who can do it and this yard has none of them. **Asked for on the twenty-ninth and refused on the twenty-ninth, and no money changed hands.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — THE READ-OUT DEVICE, USED ONCE IN THIS BATCH, AND IT FAILED.** Monday the twenty-fifth, the head of the dock, daylight, about forty people's hearing, the bar of gravel entry whole out of the yard's own book with the reason against the line, read twice. Two men of that market said afterwards to each other that it was the whole of it. **It did nothing for the man it was done for, and the yard entered that it did not and that it had not lied to him, and that those are one fact, in the second column, with the reason that a man in a market can be shown a figure and cannot be given a name.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A DIRECTIONS SITTING ON SATURDAY THE SIXTEENTH OF SEPTEMBER, AND A SITTING TO BE APPOINTED FOR THE WEEK FOLLOWING THE TWELFTH OF OCTOBER.** Ambrose Sell, clerk of the circuit office and clerk of that sitting, named, three paragraphs read out, a right to apply in writing at its own cost for a copy of any record granting no copy and no date, **and the statement in his own words that the power to seat one of two disputants is the inquiry's power and not the writ's and did not come out of the writ.** The crew's book was produced whole and unshortened a second time in about nine minutes and not one line of it was read aloud and no copy of it was made in that room. The party was un-represented and has retained nobody and has entered that it will not be represented at the next one either.
- **NEW, AND PAID — A DAY OF WORK REFUSED IN A DOORWAY BECAUSE THIS HARBOR HAS NO HOSE.** A man of the second lay booked a day for the Friday following at his own figure; the work wanted a suction hose; he was told at eight in the morning that it could not be done; the booking was refused and no money changed hands either way; and **the five marks of compressor's gland on that creditor's page since the nineteenth of May is still standing, still unpaid and still of no use whatever, because the thing after the gland is the hose.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A BOY IN A JUNE BERTH, FROM A MAN OF THE THIRD LAY WHO CAME UP A HILL IN THE RAIN AND WAS NOT ASKED ONE QUESTION, AND THE YARD WANTED TO SAY THANK YOU AND DID NOT.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — THE CONDITION NAMED ONCE, IN THE SECOND COLUMN, IN HIS OWN LEFT HAND, WITH A REASON, ON LAND, IN A DOORWAY, ON THE SUNDAY THE SEVENTEENTH, AND IT IS NOT AVAILABLE AGAIN IN THIS VOLUME OR ANY OTHER.** **THE FOUR LINES ABOVE THE NAMED LINE OF THE TWENTIETH OF JULY ARE STILL FOUR, ARE STILL NAMELESS, ARE NOT STRUCK OUT, and the count of four stands in the first column in a ship's hand with no name against it.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A WAGE THAT WAS NOT PAID, AND A SECOND WEEK FALLEN DUE.** The wage of Tuesday the fifth of September was not paid and was entered unpaid on the twelfth and not before, with the reason against the line, and **the number of weeks fallen due this year with no envelope standing against them is TWO, being the fifteenth of August and the fifth of September, and the two figures are different figures and neither of them is the other.** The wage of the twelfth was not paid either. The wages of the nineteenth and the twenty-sixth were paid, and the column went **eighteen lines and seventy-two marks · nineteen lines and seventy-six marks · twenty lines and eighty marks** and the envelopes **thirteen · fourteen · fifteen**, and the bag closed the month on the figure it opened it on.
- **NEW, AND PAID — A FIGURE IN TYPE IN A CITY BESIDE A FIGURE IN TYPE IN A CITY, AND NEITHER ABLE TO CHECK THE OTHER, AND A PENNY SPENT ON A STALL TO FIND OUT.** **This volume does not decide which of them is right and no chapter of this batch decides it.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A MOORING IN SERVICE THAT IS HARBOR GROUND AND IS NOT THIS YARD'S, AND A MAN SENT TO A TIDE GANG FOUR MEN SHORT, AND A DAY AND A HALF OF THAT GANG AS THE WHOLE OF THE PRICE.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — THE REFUSED LICENSE TAKEN OUT OF THE WRIT AND PUT BACK, AND THE THIRTY MARKS' ARITHMETIC SAID OUT LOUD ONCE IN FOUR VOLUMES: about four hundred feet of Sella Brigg's own warp came up off the bottom of this harbor on Friday the twenty-eighth of July for twenty-six marks and a day off a man of Ashtide, and this yard owes her eight shillings and tenpence of it and has not paid it, and her name and that figure went into the first column at her own asking on the Sunday the twenty-fourth and she is not on any wall.**
- **NEW, AND FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — THE FIVE ORDINARY PEOPLE THIS BATCH ADDED: Wil Tranter of the first lay, fifty-one, master of a coaster of about a hundred and ninety tons; Ailsa Rorke, surveyor, of a city, whose letterhead carries a street and a number; Denzil Hare of the third lay, about fifty; Sella Brigg, a weaver of warp of this harbor; and Ambrose Sell, who was already named and is the clerk of the sixteenth. NONE OF THEM IS AN ANTAGONIST, NONE IS ON THE LADDER, NONE KNOWS ANYTHING AND NONE IS ASKED TO KNOW ANYTHING.** Rorke in particular is a working man in his trade who wrote down the reason he entered what he entered, who offered to be wrong in a document rather than vague in one, and who asked a question in a letter that is a question and not an accusation, **and the yard has entered that it is not going to put a name on a man who has put a name on a street.**
- **NEW, AND FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — A FIGURE ENTERED IN A FILE IN A CITY THREE DAYS BEFORE ANYBODY HAD SAD IT OUT LOUD, on a line that says the surveyor had it from a man of the first lay and not from the yard.** **No Volume 04 chapter may improve on it and no chapter may have the yard say out loud that it was misreported; the yard does not get to be sorry in a street and mean it, and that is entered against itself in the first column with the date and the hour.**
- **NEW, AND FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — SIX ORDINARY WORDING FAULTS FOUND BY A REVIEWER PASS AND REPAIRED IN PLACE**: two chapters putting the crew's book into a file in a city on a *future* Saturday, three weeks after Chapter 162 put it there; a shortfall after a wage stated as a mark and twelve shillings and fourpence where twelve shillings and fourpence is the figure; a wage-count that contradicted the same chapter's own first-column entry; a day-count of rooms and a day-count of rota days; and three checkable figures in dialogue with nothing behind them. **The full disposition is in `state/continuity.md` and none of them was repaired by cutting a cost or moving a scene.**

### Ruled

- **RULED — NO POWER STAGE OF ANY KIND WAS TAKEN AND NONE MAY BE.** The Faultreader stage is spent, is not re-taken, is not improved, and **the word is on no page of these ten chapters.** He reads no seam, on a document or on anything else, and no seam settles anything, proves anything, convicts anybody or tells him what happened. **He goes in no water in this batch: two days of work on the second seam's own face with nobody in the water, a hand lead's line over the side and back four times, and no pot of air bought and none charged to any day.**
- **RULED — THE BELL IS NOT AN INSTRUMENT AND WAS NOT TOUCHED.** No boat went off the south end, no second eight marks was entered as paid, the phrase *a tongue half worn through* does not occur, the bellwright was not asked and is in no chapter of this batch as a person, and the chest was not opened and was not brought down the hill.
- **RULED — THE CUSTODY ITEMS ARE UNTOUCHED.** Three items, three numbers, **no digit quoted, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third stands, and the word does not occur once in the ten chapters of this batch.**
- **RULED — NO RETURN WAS MADE AND THERE IS NO FIFTH.** A finding of worked glass was made on no day of this batch and nothing was raised on any day of it.
- **RULED — NO PERSON, BOAT OR LEAD OF THIS YARD WENT INSIDE THE FIRST CLAUSE OF THE NOTICE OF THE THIRTY-FIRST ON ANY DAY OF THIS BATCH**, and the party entered that fact in the first column in Chapter 162 with the date. **The plate at the fifth seam was not reached, not measured, not touched and not asked about, and the removed file page and the document are reserved to Movement 3 and were not produced, quoted, summarized or described in this batch, INCLUDING SAYING THAT ONE HAPPENED.**
- **RULED — THE MAN IN A COAT IS NOT NAMED AND MAY NOT BE NAMED IN THIS VOLUME. He is in no chapter of this batch and in no room in it.**
- **RULED — AMBROSE SELL IS NAMED WHEREVER A CLERK OF THE CIRCUIT OFFICE APPEARS**, in Chapter 163 only, and the harbor authority's clerk is not a man of any consequence and keeps his own page.
- **RULED — THE SLATE WALL'S COUNTER DID NOT RESTART AND IS NINETEEN, TWENTY AND TWENTY-ONE** on the eleventh, eighteenth and twenty-fifth of September, all bare, all with the clerk keeping his own page, and no sitting held on any of them.
- **RULED — THE MARKET WALL AND THE HEARING ROOM ARE NEVER THE SAME DAY, VERIFIED A THIRD TIME.** The market wall is not touched on any day of this batch, the hearing room is used once on Saturday the sixteenth, and the market office is used once on Friday the twenty-ninth; no two of the three rooms share a day.
- **RULED — THE READ-BACK IS NOT THE READ-OUT, AND THE FIGURE FOR IT IN `state/continuity.md` WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED.** The read-out was used once, in Chapter 168, and failed. **The read-back — the ship's hand reading the day's entries back and the holder reading them back after her, nobody else in the shed — runs in FIVE of the ten chapters, 161, 163, 164, 167 and 170, with a variant of it in 168. A later batch may use the read-out once and may use the read-back on any night it likes.**
- **RULED — ALDEN MARR IS AT THE FAR BENCH ON FIVE DAYS OF THIS BATCH, being the eleventh, the twelfth, the seventeenth, the eighteenth and the twenty-fourth of September, and not asked for anything on any of them, asking nothing, and Calder Marr does not go over to him on any of them.** The figures of six days and of seven days that two state files carried are withdrawn; no fragment, no report talk, no second story, no confession.

### Open by design

- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the answer inside the delayed note.** Eleven logged occasions and two of them with no call in them, **neither advanced in this batch by arithmetic or by a measurement, no occasion entered, no measurement taken**, the delay a hand's width of a second and unmoved, and nobody in this yard and nobody in this harbor has said why. **The answer is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the release of the provisional public copy record.** It exists; the yard may ask for a copy of the copy at its own cost; the request is entered; the copy is not made and not released and no date attaches to either. **Its release is the volume's climax and is Movement 5.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the reading of the crew's book in a room.** The copy is in a file and cannot be recalled. **The reading is the volume's midpoint and is Chapters 181 and later. No chapter of Movements 1, 2 or 3 may read a line of it aloud in a room.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the page gone out of a file, and the document in which an office accepts a removal, and the phrase *risk management*.** **All three are Movement 3's and were not on any page of this batch in any form, including a clerk saying that a record is not in an office's possession and why.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — Mara Venn's break with the Registry**, which is Movement 3's turn, is a different event from her leaving this crew, and no chapter of Movements 1 or 2 fused the two.
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — who drafted the fourth paragraph of the notice.** Somebody has read a market wall of this harbor. The yard does not know who and is not going to guess.
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the three places with no joint.** Carried on the page of the twenty-fifth of August in the yard's own words: this yard does not know why the three are there and is not going to guess. **They are owed a placement in a later volume as a fact and not as an identification, and they are not the nine-line mark.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — what the shard, the packing and the marking on the mole's crown carry.** Not read, not identified, not settled. The two objects of nobody's have not moved and one tarpaulin still has a corner off it since the seventeenth of June.
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the coast road.** Described, published, doubted, priced in a city, and **not resolved and not called anything.** Five miles of it not looked at, and the yard has entered that it is not going to in the season and that this is a working reason and not a virtue.
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the custody items and the live question no paper in this harbor answers**, and the lead plug's number, and the run under this town, and the compartment, and the first echo's witness, and Neave Tarrant, nine winters, out since the twenty-eighth of February, not found, one line, and this yard has not gone.

### What Movement 3 inherits, in one place

1. **MARA VENN'S BREAK WITH THE REGISTRY IS MOVEMENT 3'S OWN TURN AND THE FIRST THING ON THE LIST.** She is in this harbor, she uses a room that is open to anybody on a Tuesday and a Friday morning, **she was asked for one thing on the twenty-ninth and refused it, and the yard cannot pay her and is not going to pretend it is going to. Her forty marks stand unpaid, first after food, since the fifteenth of October, and the six marks and two days are unpaid, unoffered and are not going to be. He still has not told her he wrote the sentence of the seventh of August down on the eighth, and that not-telling is entered and stands.** Her break with the Registry is a DIFFERENT EVENT from her leaving this crew, **no chapter of this batch shows or hints at it, and the next batch may not fuse the two.**
2. **THE PAGE GONE OUT OF A FILE, THE DOCUMENT IN WHICH AN OFFICE ACCEPTS A REMOVAL, AND THE WORD *RISK MANAGEMENT*.** All three are reserved to Movement 3 and **the phrase is not on any page of this volume before Chapter 171 and no document of that kind is produced, quoted, summarized or described before then either.** **The plate note is open and says so: the plate is in eleven feet, seen and not measured and not touched, and whatever closes that note will close it in an office and not in a shed.** **Halven Rook may be named on paper in this movement and does not appear in a room and this volume may not put him in one.**
3. **A GROSS ENTRY IN A FILE IN A CITY THAT SAYS A YARD OF MORROW QUAY DECLINES TO CERTIFY A FIGURE AND WILL NOT BE HELD TO IT**, and about four hundred people are going to read that this yard stands behind a number and does not wish to be examined, and **there is no way to get at the file, no letter was written, and the yard does not get to be sorry in a street and mean it.** The surveyor is a working man in his trade, is not an antagonist, and has not been put on any ladder.
4. **A SITTING TO BE APPOINTED FOR THE WEEK FOLLOWING THE TWELFTH OF OCTOBER, at the hearing room over the tide-gang store at ten in the morning, and a paper naming the day will be served on the party and not left with anybody.** **The party is not represented, has retained nobody, has entered that it will not be represented at the next one either, and the hearing room and the market wall are never the same day and are verified three times.**
5. **THE CREW'S BOOK IS IN A FILE IN A CITY AND CANNOT BE RECALLED, and the reading of it in a room is the volume's midpoint and is Chapters 181 and later.** Two days of work this month went to holding a book up whole in a room and reading not one line of it out.
6. **A MARK AND TWELVE SHILLINGS AND FOURPENCE IN A BAG, the same figure on the eleventh and on the thirtieth of September**, three marks owing to a man of Ashtide unmoved since the eighth of August, seven pence owing to this yard's own shipwright, five marks of gland unpaid and useless, thirty-one on a note not re-written with three counts unmoved, nothing in a tin since the twenty-eighth of April, eight marks and a day of a boat to the bellwright, eight shillings and tenpence to a woman of this harbor with her name on the figure and not on any wall, forty marks to a person of no office, forty marks to a boy of twenty-two, and **a wage of four on Tuesday the third of October.** No office in this harbor has paid this yard a mark since the eighth of May.
7. **A NINTH ACKNOWLEDGEMENT AND NO TENTH, and the party entered in the first column on the twelfth of September that it did not go and did not ask and why.** The reeve is in no chapter of this batch as a person and was not asked for anything.
8. **THE THIRD COLUMN, THE BOY, AND THE PENCIL, AND THE ONE NAMING OF THE CONDITION SPENT.** The third column has not been filled since the first of August and the shipwright has said out loud, in the first column, why she did not fill it. **The one naming Volume 04 was allowed was used on Sunday the seventeenth and is not available again. The four nameless lines above the named line of the twentieth of July are still four and are not struck out.**
9. **THE COAST ROAD, THE PILOT, AND THE KETTLE.** Both figures for the bar of gravel are in type in a city side by side, neither is withdrawn, **this volume does not decide which of them is right and Movement 3 may not decide it either.** The refused license is carried as a cost and is not re-decided. The Kettle was not looked at and five miles of that coast was not looked at.
10. **A HARBOR POORER ON THE THIRTIETH OF SEPTEMBER THAN IT WAS ON THE ELEVENTH, and the cistern walked on none of its eight charges in September.** One herring berth out, the north berth out, a light not on, a tide gang four short, a fire party's hose dead, a hard at Ashtide unanswered since the ninth of November, nineteen winters in a list in a city, and the *Sea Wren* at the pile with this party not having thanked him.
11. **And the six open threads, unchanged in number.** Mara Venn's break with the Registry; the third column, the boy and the pencil; the nine-line mark, which closes in Volume 5; the question about who changed the rule; the second season of the writ and the first of March and the unpaid wages and the three marks; and the five bells at five and a half fathoms with the four people of the Sill who have no boat.

## Volume 04, Batch 0003 (Chapters 171–180) — as written, Movement 3, the woman whose work is being used, and the file with a page out of it

**34,984 words across ten chapters, Thursday the first to Tuesday the twentieth of October 2026, re-measured after this batch's review repairs, which took it from 34,984 to 35,190. MOVEMENT 3 IS COMPLETE, THE TURN HAS LANDED, AND ALL THREE OF THE MOVEMENT'S RESERVED BEATS ARE PAID ONCE EACH AND ARE NOW SPENT. The full accounting is `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0003 as written (Chapters 171–180), Volume 04*, the duplication audit is § 10 of that section, and **THE REPAIRS ARE DISPOSED OF IN FULL AT § *Batch 0003 (Volume 04) review repairs*, WHICH IS THE AUTHORITY FOR EVERY FIGURE, DATE AND COUNT CORRECTED IN THIS FILE AND IN THE OTHER THREE.** The six open threads are the six open threads and this batch opened no seventh. NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO SCENE WAS CUT, NO COST WAS DROPPED AND NO PLANNED PLOT WAS ALTERED.

### Carried and moved

- **CARRIED, AND PAID — MARA VENN'S BREAK WITH THE REGISTRY. IT IS THIS MOVEMENT AND IT IS A DIFFERENT EVENT FROM HER LEAVING THIS CREW, AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH FUSED THE TWO.** She found out on Friday the ninth of October at about half past ten in the morning, in a room off the fish market that is open to anybody, from a woman of fifty-eight who is a mentor of hers. **She left the Registry in her own words, in a letter of four lines in the ordinary post on Sunday the eleventh at about half past two, at a penny out of her own pocket, before anybody asked her and before she came up the hill, and she told this party about it after it had gone to the post and not before, and entered that the order of those two things was her decision and not theirs.** The letter says she will not be cited, quoted, produced, or asked again by that office or by any office of it, and that no form, sheet, notation, hand or date of hers is to be used to support an account she did not write and has not read, and that she is not resigning anything **because there is nothing to resign from**, and that a letter cannot stop a thing that has already been done and that she has written it anyway. **SHE DID NOT COME BACK TO THIS CREW AND IS NOT ON IT AND WAS NOT ASKED FOR ANYTHING BY IT IN THIS BATCH AND IS NOT GOING TO BE. HER FORTY MARKS STAND UNPAID, FIRST AFTER FOOD, SINCE THE FIFTEENTH OF OCTOBER, AND ARE NOT REDUCED, AND THE SIX MARKS AND TWO DAYS WERE NOT OFFERED, PAID OR REDUCED, AND A FAIR COPY OF FOUR SHEETS OF HER OWN WRITING WAS CHARGED TO HER AT A PENNY A SHEET AND PAID, AND THAT PENNY IS NOT THE FORTY MARKS.**
- **CARRIED, AND PAID — THE SPINE.** **He still has not told her.** A decision to tell her was entered in the second column on Saturday the third of October and the reason he did not, and the fact that he had not told her, were entered in the second column on the fourth, and on Monday the twelfth she came up this hill and told him four things about a card and a file and a plate and **he did not tell her, and the second column says the reason he did not is the reason of the fourth of October and that there is nothing new to add to it and he is not going to invent a second one**, and on the seventeenth **she said out loud in the shed that she is not going to ask him and had him enter on one line that she did not ask and that he did not tell, with a reason against the line that is about him and not about her, so that in about four years anybody can read that line and work out that a man of a yard had a thing in his own book for six weeks and a woman knew there was a thing in it and neither of them ever said so out loud.** **THE SPINE MOVED TWICE IN THIS BATCH AND IT MOVED BECAUSE OF HER. HE ASKED HER FOR NOTHING. SHE ASKED HIM FOR NOTHING.**
- **CARRIED, AND ADVANCED — the third column, the boy and the pencil.** **The third column was not filled on any day of this batch and the ship's hand said out loud on Saturday the seventeenth that she had nine sentences and did not write them, and gave the reason, and the reason is that the column is a page where a man wrote down what he said when he was not in the boat, and that if those nine sentences went in it this yard would be holding a page about what a woman said to it in a doorway, and that in about four years there is going to be a page in a city that says what a yard of Morrow Quay knew in October and anybody can read it.** **Bevin Rook is in no chapter of this batch as a person. The three nots of the eighth of June stand. The head of the third column keeps his hand. Forty marks stand whole and are not reduced and are not going to be.**
- **CARRIED, AND UNTOUCHED — the nine-line mark.** Not referred to, not named, not explained, and not asked about in this batch. **It closes in Volume 5 and not before, and the three places with no joint are a different thing and were not converted into either, and the nine fastenings are not a mark of nine joined lines and are not on a page of this batch at all.**
- **CARRIED, AND UNTOUCHED — the question about who changed the rule.** Asked in a market in this harbor on Sunday the thirteenth of August and not answered. **NOTHING IN THIS BATCH ANSWERS IT AND NOTHING IN THIS BATCH ASKS IT AGAIN, and a document in which an office accepts a removal came within four hundred yards of answering it and did not, and the yard entered that on the eighth of October that a number in a book in a shed is not a change of politics in an office and is not going to be made into one by anybody in that shed.**
- **CARRIED, AND PAID TWICE — the second season of the writ, the first of March, the unpaid wages and the three marks.** The second season is a named absence and **nothing in this batch touches it, grants it, refuses it or deals with it, and the paper served on the party on the fifteenth says so on its own face for the fourth time in ten weeks.** **On Tuesday the sixth the wage of four marks did not go in and the envelope was not made, the third Tuesday running, and the number of weeks fallen due this year with no envelope standing against them is THREE, and all three are entered in the same breath in every place the figure is given.** **THREE MARKS ARE STILL OWING TO A MAN OF ASHTIDE FOR THE EIGHTH OF AUGUST AND NOT A SHILLING OF IT HAS BEEN PAID, he was named once in this volume and is not named again, and no chapter of this batch asks him a question, and the figure is not reduced.**
- **CARRIED, AND UNTOUCHED — the five bells at about five and a half fathoms inside the four hundred yards.** Not raised, not offered, not argued, not promised, and not mentioned as anything anybody can do. The four people of the Sill hold a limit that stands, the holder did not ask them to take it off, and they are named nowhere as persons in this batch and were asked nothing.

### Opened or advanced by this batch

- **NEW, AND THE MOVEMENT'S TURN, AND IT IS PAID — A WITHDRAWAL COUNTERFOIL IN THE REGISTRY'S OWN HAND WITH THE REASON *RISK MANAGEMENT* ENTERED AGAINST IT, DATED THE SECOND OF SEPTEMBER 2026.** A commissioned officer of the Crown Navy applied by letter of the first for one leaf out of the Registry's file of the ASTER GANNET. The Registry agreed. The leaf is not to be produced and a copy is held. **The yard did not have this from any office. It had it because a copyist of fifty-eight kept the counterfoil in a box under her counter, and she handed it over at a penny-a-sheet counter because she was taught by a woman of thirty to write a leaf number at the head of a sheet and because a sheet without a number is a sheet nobody can check.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — THE ACCOUNT THAT OFFICE'S WORK IS BEING USED TO VALIDATE.** Leaf 62 is a conservator's own refusal of the eleventh of November 2025, in her own hand, on four grounds, with her trade against her name and a date and the office's stamp on it because she would not let it leave without one. **A copy of it was made and the copy is to be read with leaf 88, which is a page she has never seen and which nobody in this harbor has seen.** She did not write leaf 88. She wrote leaf 62, and an office read her page as support for an account the office wrote itself. **The first of her four grounds is that the office will not countersign an instrument putting a person inside a closure that the same office's own notice created, and that is the whole of the plate note's mechanism and it is in her hand and it is in a file in a city and this yard has not got it and is not going to ask for it.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A DOCUMENT IN WHICH AN OFFICE ACCEPTS A REMOVAL, IN A DOCUMENT SERVED ON THE PARTY, IN A DOORWAY, ON THE FIFTEENTH OF OCTOBER.** A paper of six paragraphs from the clerk of the circuit office states that the Registry file of the ASTER GANNET is before the inquiry, that one leaf is called for and is not produced, and that the reason of the non-production is on a form of the Registry's own dated the second of September, a copy of which is on the record, and that the form is not before the sitting and is not to be produced to the party. **THE YARD NOW KNOWS ON A PAPER SERVED ON IT THAT A PAGE IS GONE, AND CANNOT CHECK WHAT WAS ON IT, AND THE PROOF IS A FORM THE OFFICE SAYS EXISTS AND WILL NOT PRODUCE.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — HALVEN ROOK, MAGISTRATE OF THAT CIRCUIT, NAMED ON PAPER AND NOWHERE ELSE.** The name stands at the foot of the original of the paper served on the party, in a file in a city, and is not on the copy, and the clerk said out loud that he has not been in this harbor and is not going to be, and that the truth about where the name comes from is that it is on a paper and not in a room. **HE APPEARS IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AS A PERSON AND IN NO ROOM IN IT.**
- **NEW, AND PAID, AND IT IS A COST AND NOT A WIN — ELEVEN WORDS IN A RECORD IN A CITY.** A clerk of the circuit office asks in a room whether there is any person in this harbor whose hand is on a page of that file and who is not an officer of any office in it. The holder answers yes, and says she is not on the crew and is not of the yard and is not a holder and is not on the four names, and that he is not going to give her name and is not going to say that she is in this harbor. **The clerk writes it down, reads it back, and enters it. It is in a record in a city. It cannot be got off a page. In about a fortnight about four hundred people can read that the yard of Morrow Quay has a person and will not say who she is, and the party said in a room that it would rather have that than have the record say this harbor produced nothing.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A WOMAN OF THIS HARBOR'S COPYING TRADE, PERNEL SAYE, FIFTY-EIGHT, WITH A COUNTER IN A ROOM OFF THE FISH MARKET THAT IS OPEN TO ANYBODY.** She copied eleven sheets for the circuit office's copying room on Friday the eighth of September and has not been paid for them yet and is not going up to ask, because she will be paid on the twentieth. She has been up the stairs to that room twice in her life and is not going a third time. She keeps nine years of withdrawal counterfoils in a box under her counter and has never thrown one away. **She taught a woman of thirty to write a leaf number at the head of a sheet, and that is the whole of what she taught her and it is the whole of what was needed.** She came up this hill on the first of October unasked and at her own cost and was not thanked and did not ask to be. **She is not a villain, is not on the ladder, is not given a title, a uniform, a commission or a motive that is not a copyist's, and knows nothing and was not asked to know anything.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A MAN OF THE FIRST LAY OF THIS HARBOR, OSWIN REDE, ABOUT FIFTY**, who asked a room in the market office on the thirteenth for the sense of a form with a tide conservator's hand on it, and the room could not answer him, and came up this hill on the fourteenth and was told the truth in about nine sentences and sent down the hill, and said he is not owed anything by this yard and that nobody in this yard has asked him for a thing. **HE IS SENT DOWN A HILL ONCE BY THIS PARTY, AND HE SAYS HE CAME UP TWICE AND ASKED A MAN IN A DOORWAY ON THE SATURDAY AS WELL, AND THAT IS HIS OWN ACCOUNT AND THE YARD'S BOOK SAYS ONE. THE COST OF IT IS ENTERED AS A FACT ABOUT THE HARBOR AND NOT AS A VIRTUE.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — SHE IS NOT COMING TO THE MARKET OFFICE OF THIS TOWN ON A TUESDAY OR A FRIDAY MORNING ANY MORE**, at her own asking, with her own reason, which is not going to be given, and which anybody who wants may ask her for and be refused twice. **THE YARD DID NOT ARRANGE IT AND IS NOT GOING TO UN-ARRANGE IT, AND THE READER IS GIVEN THE MEASURE OF THE CHANGE IN A ROOM OPEN TO ANYBODY WHERE NINE PEOPLE COULD NOT ANSWER A QUESTION THAT THE ONE PERSON WHO COULD HAVE ANSWERED IT WAS NOT PRESENT TO ANSWER.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — THE LINE REFUSED TWICE.** On the twenty-ninth of September at a figure and at nothing in a market office in eleven people's hearing, and again on the twelfth of October on a mole, and the reason given both times, and the price unchanged: **a line of hers in a book of the yard would be a document, and the Registry's page is a document, and a man in a room in October holds two documents and only one of them carries a seal, and the one without the seal is hers. That is how a person of no office becomes a thing a person with an office can use.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A MAN OF THE FIRST LAY REFUSES A SURVEYOR IN A CITY TO PUT HIS WORD UNDER A LINE ABOUT WATER.** Wil Tranter, fifty-one, wrote to Ailsa Rorke on the Sunday night, gave his reason in full, brought the letter up the hill on the Tuesday unasked, would not take a shilling for it, and said out loud that he would rather it was on the page that the yard offered and he would not have it. **THE YARD CANNOT USE THE LETTER AND ENTERED THAT IT CANNOT, AND THE SURVEYOR'S ENTRY STANDS WITH FOUR WORDS IN IT THAT THIS YARD NEVER SAID, AND NEITHER FIGURE FOR THE BAR OF GRAVEL IS WITHDRAWN AND THIS VOLUME DOES NOT DECIDE WHICH IS RIGHT.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A WAGE THAT WAS NOT PAID AND THE FIGURE OF THREE.** Tuesday the sixth of October, the third Tuesday in a row, no envelope made, and the number of weeks fallen due this year with no envelope standing against them is **three** — the fifteenth of August, the fifth of September and the sixth of October — entered in the same breath in every place the figure is given, and the wage column at twenty-three lines and ninety-two marks and seventeen envelopes at the close of the batch.
- **NEW, AND PAID — FOUR PENCE, THE POSTAGE OF ONE LETTER IN THE ORDINARY POST, AND IT IS THE ONLY MONEY THIS YARD HAS SPENT ON ITSELF IN TWENTY DAYS.** The bag closes the month at **a mark and twelve shillings** against **a mark and twelve shillings and fourpence** at the open, and the difference is the postage and the whole of the cost of the month. **Nessa Pike's seven pence for the postage of the letter of the twentieth of August stands unpaid and is not paid out of the bag, and the two figures are not on the same line and never have been, and she said in the shed on the second that if the two ever are, she will take the book off the bench.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A DAY OF WORK REFUSED BY THIS YARD, AND A DAY OF WORK THAT WAS THERE TO BE BOOKED AND WAS NOT BOOKED.** A day of about three marks on a wharf was offered for Monday the twelfth and refused, and the reason was given out loud and was a bad one and a working one: a crew of two and a half cannot lose a Monday in the week before a sitting, and there is nobody to stand in for it and nobody is going to be hired to stand in for it. And on the seventh a day on the second seam's own face was there to be booked and was not, for a reason of this harbor's and not of this yard's — the third lay short, the tide gang four short, one herring berth of two out, a started plank — **and it was not entered as *no work found*, because that would be a lie and a flattering one and this column is not the place for either.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A TIN CUP ON THE END OF THE FAR BENCH, ON THE EIGHTEENTH OF OCTOBER AT ABOUT ELEVEN IN THE MORNING.** The holder put it there and went back to his own bench and said nothing. The man did not turn round and did not drink it and did not refuse it and said no word. It was still there on Monday morning at nine and not there at four in the afternoon. Neither brother saw who took it and neither brother has said a word about it and neither brother is going to. **The holder entered on the Sunday that he has done that once and is not going to do it again, and why: a cup of water on the end of a bench is a question in a language this party made up, and this party does not get to make up a language for a man who has not been asked anything. IT IS NOT A FRAGMENT, NOT A REPORT, NOT A SECOND STORY AND NOT A CONFESSION.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — A CRESCENDO IN A SHED ON THE TWENTIETH: FIVE THINGS THE PARTY CAN DO ABOUT A DOCUMENT, AND FOUR OF THEM COST MONEY IT HAS NOT GOT, AND THE FIFTH IS A ROOM IT HAS ALREADY BEEN IN AND SAID A DIFFERENT THING IN.** And the answer it gave itself, which is **that a hearing is a room a party may stand in without paying for it, and that what comes out of such a room goes on a page in a city and cannot be got off, and that the holder has been treating the second of those as if it were the first since the seventh of July, and that a wall has nothing in it you can be made to answer for and a room has a person in it and a clerk with a pen.**
- **NEW, AND PAID — THE VOLUME'S FORWARD PULL, ENTERED IN THE FIRST COLUMN ON THE TWENTIETH OF OCTOBER.** **The reading of this yard's own crew's book in a room HAS NEVER HAPPENED.** It was produced whole in a hearing room on the sixteenth of October and not one line of it was read out of it, and a schedule was read into the record instead, and a clerk asked one question and it was not about the book. **IT IS COMING, THE PARTY HAS ENTERED THAT IT KNOWS IT IS COMING AND IS NOT GOING TO SOFTEN A LINE AND IS NOT GOING TO LEAVE ONE OUT, AND THE WEEK OF IT IS NOT IN THIS PARTY'S KEEPING AND IS NOT GOING TO BE. IT IS CHAPTERS 181 AND LATER AND IT IS THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT.**

### Ruled

- **RULED — NO POWER STAGE OF ANY KIND WAS TAKEN AND NONE MAY BE.** The Faultreader stage is spent, is not re-taken, is not improved, and **the word is on no page of these ten chapters.** He reads no seam, on a document or on anything else, and no seam settles anything, proves anything, convicts anybody or tells him what happened. **HE GOES IN NO WATER IN THIS BATCH: two days of work on the second seam's own face with nobody in the water, a hand lead's line over the side and back four times, and no pot of air bought and none charged to any day.**
- **RULED — THE CONDITION IS NAMED NOWHERE IN THIS BATCH.** The bare fact is carried twice, in the second column, with a reason against each line, and without a name. **The four lines above the named line of the twentieth of July are still four, are still nameless, and are not struck out, and the count of four stands in the first column in a ship's hand with no name against it, and the one naming Volume 04 was allowed was spent in Chapter 164 and is not available again.**
- **RULED — THE BELL IS NOT AN INSTRUMENT AND WAS NOT TOUCHED.** No boat went off the south end, no second eight marks was entered as paid, the phrase *a tongue half worn through* does not occur, the bellwright was not asked and is in no chapter of this batch as a person, and the chest was not opened and was not brought down the hill.
- **RULED — THE LEAD PLUG IS UNTOUCHED.** Not asked about, not printed, not opened, and not brought down the hill. The undertaking of the second of July binds the yard and not the office and not the market and not the boy.
- **RULED — THE CUSTODY ITEMS ARE UNTOUCHED.** Three items, three numbers, no digit quoted, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third stands, and **the word does not occur once in the ten chapters of this batch.** The live question — what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material — is not answered and is not put to anybody.
- **RULED — NO RETURN WAS MADE AND THERE IS NO FIFTH.** A finding of worked glass was made on no day of this batch and nothing was raised on any day of it, and the reporting clause was not touched and no day was reported to any office.
- **RULED — NO PERSON, BOAT OR LEAD OF THIS YARD WENT INSIDE THE FIRST CLAUSE OF THE NOTICE OF THE THIRTY-FIRST ON ANY DAY OF THIS BATCH.** The plate at the fifth seam was not reached, not measured, not touched and not asked about, and the eight-clause plan of the seventeenth of January was not reopened, and **the plate note is OPEN and is not required to close in this batch.**
- **RULED — THE MAN IN A COAT IS NOT NAMED AND MAY NOT BE NAMED IN THIS VOLUME. HE IS IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AND IN NO ROOM IN IT.** The commissioned officer of the Crown Navy who applied by letter for leaf 62 on the first of September is also not named, not described, and not guessed at, and the yard entered that it does not know and has not asked and is not going to guess.
- **RULED — AMBROSE SELL IS NAMED WHEREVER A CLERK OF THE CIRCUIT OFFICE APPEARS**, in Chapter 178 only, and the harbor authority's clerk is not a man of any consequence and keeps his own page. The press-keeper at the market office and the copyist are working women and not officers of anything.
- **RULED — THE SLATE WALL'S COUNTER DID NOT RESTART AND IS TWENTY-TWO, TWENTY-THREE AND TWENTY-FOUR** on the fifth, twelfth and nineteenth of October, all bare, all with the clerk keeping his own page, and no sitting held on any of them. The first of October is a Thursday and is not counted.
- **RULED — THE MARKET WALL AND THE HEARING ROOM ARE NEVER THE SAME DAY, VERIFIED A FOURTH TIME.** The market wall is not touched on any day of this batch, the hearing room is used once on Friday the sixteenth, and the market office is used once on Tuesday the thirteenth; no two of the three rooms share a day.
- **RULED — THE READ-OUT DEVICE WAS NOT USED IN THIS BATCH AND ITS ONE USE PER BATCH IS CARRIED FORWARD. THE READ-BACK IS NOT THE READ-OUT AND RAN IN FOUR OF THE TEN CHAPTERS, 172, 178, 179 AND 180, and not in 173, 175, 176 or 177.** A later batch may use the read-out once and may use the read-back on any night it likes. **THE FIGURE OF SEVEN THAT THIS FILE CARRIED BEFORE THE REVIEW REPAIRS IS WITHDRAWN AND NAMED AS WITHDRAWN HERE; the method is a plain string search for *read back* and *read-back* across the ten chapters and it returns four.**
- **RULED — ALDEN MARR IS AT THE FAR BENCH ON SIX DAYS OF THIS BATCH, BEING THE SECOND, FIFTH, EIGHTH, TWELFTH, EIGHTEENTH AND NINETEENTH OF OCTOBER, AND ON NO OTHER DAY IN IT,** not asked for anything on any of the six and asking nothing on any of the six, and Calder Marr did not go over to him on any of the six. The six days are entered in the first column in Chapter 180 and agree with the prose in the six chapters that place him there — 171, 173, 174, 176, 179 and 180. **THE FIGURE OF SEVEN AND THE DAYS OF THE FIRST AND THE SIXTEENTH ARE WITHDRAWN AND NAMED AS WITHDRAWN: the earlier sentence in this file claimed that the list agreed with the prose in all seven chapters, and that was false, because no chapter of this batch places him on the first or on the sixteenth and Chapter 171 places him on the second, which the list left out. This is the second time a far-bench figure in a state file has had to be corrected against the chapters, and the rule the Batch 0002 review left behind is the rule that catches it: A FIRST-COLUMN ENTRY AND THE PROSE HAVE TO AGREE WITH EACH OTHER ABOUT HOW MANY DAYS A MAN SAT ON A BENCH, AND A NEXT BATCH MAY NOT INHERIT A FIGURE THAT THE CHAPTERS DO NOT SUPPORT.**

### Open by design

- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the answer inside the delayed note.** Eleven logged occasions and two of them with no call in them, **neither advanced in this batch by arithmetic or by a measurement, no occasion entered, no measurement taken**, the delay a hand's width of a second and unmoved, and nobody in this yard and nobody in this harbor has said why. **The answer is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the release of the provisional public copy record.** It exists; the yard may apply in writing at its own cost for a copy of any record; **it has not applied, and the first column of Chapter 178 carries the reason: a copy of a record is a thing nobody in this harbor can check, and the party said that out loud in a market in July and has not said it better since.** **The copy is not made, not released, not printed, not quoted and no date attaches to any of it. ITS RELEASE IS THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX AND IS MOVEMENT 5.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the reading of the crew's book in a room.** It has never happened. It happened everywhere else three times and the crew's book was produced whole and not one line of it was read out of it in any of the three. **THE READING IS THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT AND IS CHAPTERS 181 AND LATER. NO CHAPTER OF MOVEMENTS 1, 2 OR 3 HAS READ A LINE OF THAT BOOK ALOUD IN A ROOM, HAS PRODUCED A COPY OF A RECORD IN A ROOM, OR HAS PUT A SEAM ON A DOCUMENT.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — who drafted the fourth paragraph of the notice of the tenth of September.** Somebody has read a market wall of this harbor. The yard does not know who and is not going to guess. **This batch does not remark on that question in any mouth.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the three places with no joint.** Carried on the page of the twenty-fifth of August in the yard's own words: this yard does not know why the three are there and is not going to guess. **They are owed a placement in a later volume as a fact and not as an identification, and they are not the nine-line mark, and this batch did not ask them and did not convert anything into anything.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — what the shard, the packing and the marking on the mole's crown carry.** Not read, not identified, not settled. The two objects of nobody's have not moved and one tarpaulin still has a corner off it since the seventeenth of June, and both were counted once in every chapter that mentions them and never twice in the same chapter.
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the coast road.** Described, published, doubted, priced in a press in a city, and **not resolved and not called anything.** Five miles of it not looked at, the Kettle not looked at, and the refused license not re-decided and not argued.
